📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Arrivé chez les Verts au dernier mercato d'hiver, Marten-Chris Paalberg, 18 ans seulement et international A à moins de 17 ans avec sa sélection Estonienne s'inscrit dans la durée avec les Verts.

Le club vient de communiquer sur la prolongation de l'attaquant jusqu'en 2030.

Une belle marque de confiance pour un joueur qui, aura d'abord du se remettre d'une blessure avant de pouvoir porter la tunique verte avec la réserve depuis seulement quelques semaines.

Rappelé en sélection pendant cette trêve internationale, celui qui a débuté dans l'élite Estonienne à seulement 15 ans devra patienter encore un peu avant de débuter sa carrière avec les pros et participer activement au développement de l'équipe comme il l'exprime sur le site officiel : "Je me sens bien intégré, sur le terrain comme en dehors, et je suis pleinement déterminé à poursuivre ma progression pour contribuer aux ambitions du club sur le long terme. J'ai hâte de poursuivre ce chapitre."

Cole Hauser Is Going Undercover for the FBI. (Sort of.)

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The Dutton Ranch actor will star in a new series about a real-life FBI agent who infiltrated neo-Nazi groups.

Cole Hauser went toe to toe with Texas as Dutton Ranch’s Rip Wheeler. Now, he’ll take on white supremacist groups, drug traffickers, and motorcycle gangs in a new series based on former undercover FBI agent Scott Payne’s 2025 memoir, Code Name: Pale Horse: How I Went Undercover to Expose America's Nazis.

According to Deadline, the Yellowstone actor is developing the project with members of Constantin Film (Resident Evil), with an aim to star in the lead role as well. The story would follow Payne, or a character much like him, who spent two decades working undercover in extremist groups to bring them to justice from the inside.

One scene from Payne’s memoir includes a Halloween night in which he had to slaughter and drink the blood of a goat with a violent neo-Nazi group in Georgia. He’s burned crosses with the Ku Klux Klan and smuggled drugs across the border—all to bring in hundreds of arrests for the FBI.

“Feeling so blessed! Let’s go!!!!” Payne reacted to the news on social media.

Dubbed “The Hillbilly Donnie Brasco,” Payne’s memoir also details the struggles of undercover work, as well as how he managed his marriage, his faith, and fatherhood throughout some of the most dangerous operations in FBI history.

“These are the type of stories that I am drawn to as a producer and as an actor,” Hauser told Deadline. “Scott Payne is a real-life hero, with the kind of moxie and edge you would need to risk your life to fight evil. I am looking forward to producing this movie and getting a chance to bring him and his story to life on the screen.”

Elsewhere, Hauser is also working on a series about the origins of modern-day poker with Sylvester Stallone titled Blood Aces: The Wild Ride of Benny Binion. He told Esquire in 2023 that he was in the early stages of a series where he and Matthew McConaughey would play brothers, written by True Detective’s Nick Pizzolatto. (Brothers may have gotten in the way of that one.) But Hauser’s also gearing up for the release of Dutton Ranch season 2, which will likely air sometime in 2027.

Arbitrum (ARB), one of the leading Layer-2 scaling solutions on Ethereum, is currently navigating a phase where strong ecosystem relevance contrasts with prolonged price weakness. While the network continues to play a key role in DeFi and Layer-2 infrastructure, its price action has remained under sustained pressure.

Following an extended downtrend, ARB is now stabilizing near lower demand zones, suggesting that selling momentum may be gradually easing. However, the absence of strong upside movement indicates that the market remains in a transitional phase rather than a confirmed recovery.

This creates a critical question: is Arbitrum forming a long-term base after capitulation, or does the structure still reflect weak demand?

With that in mind, let’s take a closer look at Arbitrum native token, ARB, and explore its price outlook for 2026, 2027, and 2030

Arbitrum is down 12.06% to $0.156 in 24 hours, falling much more sharply than Bitcoin and the broader crypto market. This points to stronger selling pressure on ARB.

Hotter-than-expected U.S. inflation also added pressure to the crypto market. Meanwhile, U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $201.08 million in net outflows on November 9. These developments may have added to selling pressure.

ARB remains under pressure below $0.1613. A move back above this level could open the way toward $0.1702. However, a break below $0.1500 could lead to further losses.

Traders are also watching the December 8–9 Fed meeting and December 10 CPI report for fresh market signals

Arbitrum is entering the final quater of 2026 with a strong focus on scaling, predictable fees, cross-chain infrastructure, and institutional adoption. The ArbOS Dia upgrade has already improved Arbitrum’s fee-pricing system, while newer upgrades are adding compliance tools and greater capacity for dedicated chains.

The ecosystem is also expanding its revenue base. In H1 2026, ArbitrumDAO generated $6.19 million in income, with protocol revenue margins above 97%. Under the Arbitrum Expansion Program, participating chains return 10% of net protocol revenue to the Arbitrum ecosystem. Robinhood Chain also launched its Arbitrum-powered mainnet in July 2026.

Looking ahead, stronger institutional adoption, rising network activity, and continued development of Arbitrum’s chain ecosystem could support ARB’s long-term recovery. However, ARB’s price will still depend on broader market conditions and actual network growth.

Arbitrum (ARB) has staged a strong recovery from its long-term downtrend, with the chart showing a sharp move from the $0.07–$0.10 support zone to around $0.24. The current setup suggests buyers have regained short-term momentum, although the price is approaching an important resistance area.

The chart marks $0.3359 as the first major resistance. A strong daily close above this level could open the path toward the next major resistance at $0.6227. On the downside, $0.068 is the key long-term support, while the recent breakout zone around $0.20 could act as near-term support.

Recent technical data also shows ARB trading above its major moving averages, although RSI is in overbought territory, indicating that a short-term pullback remains possible.

The ArbOS Dia upgrade and growing revenue from Robinhood’s Orbit chain could strengthen Arbitrum’s ecosystem, potentially pushing ARB toward $0.6226.

With the final legacy token unlocks ending in March, reduced selling pressure could improve ARB’s market stability and support a move toward $1.25.

As Universal Intents expands cross-chain transactions and supports larger settlement volumes, stronger network adoption could drive ARB toward $2.41.

A broader crypto market recovery, combined with rising institutional adoption of tokenized real-world assets, could lift ARB toward $4.

As zero-knowledge technology and cross-network settlement mature, Arbitrum’s expanding Layer-2 infrastructure could support ARB reaching $10.50.

Bitcoin, Gold & Productive Land: Your Shield Against Inflation: Anthony Pompliano

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Popular crypto commentator Anthony Pompliano has shortlisted Bitcoin, Gold and Productive land to combat inflation in the uncertain times ahead. He has warned multiple times that the dollarized economy is in decline, and consumers need to protect their wealth by investing in scarce, useful assets that will hold their value well.

He used the term “money printer” in his tweet, which has often been used satirically to attack central banks for their money-printing spree. The short tweet sums up the ethos of the crypto-savvy investor class who have been clamoring for better wealth management for the better part of the last decade and a half. He, along with cypherpunks and other maximalists, believes the best defense against the onslaught of inflationary forces is to invest in assets that cannot be conjured out of thin air.

This is not the first time Pompliano has stated his preference for these three assets. Bitcoin is obviously the asset of choice for this particular demographic that emerged after the devastating 2008 financial crisis.

Gold is obviously another major asset that has withstood the test of time and has appreciated strongly in the last decade or so. Productive land is another asset with a strong future as the world faces uncertain times, with food insecurity at record levels.

The timing of the tweet is crucial, as investors look for orthodox and unorthodox ways to preserve or increase their wealth over time. The effects of large-scale stimulus and debt accumulation cannot be understated. Inflation is reducing the value of savings, and the interest-based economy can hardly keep up with it, let alone deliver a steady net increase.

Twitter responses to the post varied a lot. A large section of Pompliano’s followers said it was a good trio of assets and highlighted their explosive growth over the years. However, others criticized that it wasn’t easy to hold any of these three assets.

Gold requires custody, and holding it individually can be risky, too, with no yield. Bitcoin, on the other hand, requires a relatively tech-savvy mindset and can be volatile at times. Thirdly, land requires management, faces liquidity constraints, and can be affected by local economic or environmental factors. It is not a walk in the park to take advantage of any of these assets.

However, given the uncertain future, they are certainly worth considering, as we all know one thing: fiat is likely to get worse over time.

Microsoft lobbies against potential ban on Chinese optical networking gear for AI data centers as FCC mulls restricting imports

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The supply of this tiny module (or lack thereof) could derail the hundreds of massive data centers planned in the U.S.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Microsoft, the tech giant that is building 35 data centers around the U.S. and has plans for 134 more, has reportedly met with the White House Office of Science and Technology Policy and the National Security Council as the FCC is considering an import ban on Chinese optical transceivers. According to Reuters, the company, alongside the Information Technology Industry Council (ITI), is lobbying against the move.

“It really threatens to arguably grind (the data center rollout) to a halt,” ITI Executive Vice President of Policy and General Counsel John Miller said. He also added, “Those chips, without optical transceivers, literally could just sit there waiting around unutilized until you actually have the transceivers to essentially connect them together,” referring to the AI chips that data centers use.

Optical transceivers are tiny modules plugged into hardware that convert their electrical signals into light signals, allowing data to be transmitted as light pulses across fiber optics. Data centers increasingly use optical transceivers and fiber optics to transmit data between AI GPUs and server racks, as they’re faster, offer lower latency, require less power, and generate less heat compared to traditional copper connections. However, Chinese companies currently supply 66% of the global market, meaning banning them from supplying data centers in the U.S. would more than halve the available supply, thereby exacerbating the various shortages that have been pummeling the industry.

However, there are concerns that some of these transceivers could be compromised by Beijing and be used to steal data, install malware, or remotely disrupt American data centers, which is why the FCC is considering the restriction, just like how it banned Huawei from the U.S. telecommunications network. But unlike Huawei, which had many established competitors in the telecommunications hardware industry, only two U.S.-based companies produce optical transceivers that data centers need — Coherent and Lumentum. While these firms could produce optical transceivers to compete with Chinese offerings, they simply do not have the scale to make up for the shortfall if the FCC bans Chinese imports today.

One compromise floated by the Trump administration is that it would only ban Chinese optical transceivers capable of hitting 3.2 terabits per second. While this technology already exists, its production isn’t expected to expand until 2028, with installation not beginning until 2029. This should give American producers time to ramp up their own output, although the suggestion is still under consideration.

Grayscale pledges $1 million alongside Zcash ad blitz as token and ETF fall 12%

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Grayscale has awarded $400,000 to four Zcash initiatives as its ETF records over $15 million in November net outflows.

Grayscale kicked off a Zcash advertising campaign on Tuesday with a new website, a front-page Wall Street Journal wrap, and more grants to groups working on the crypto's development.

The WSJ ad includes a zebra-striped wrap and a full-page advertisement inside promoting Grayscale's Zcash ETF Grayscale Zcash Trust ETF (ZCSH). The asset manager also launched ZcashInvest.com as an educational resource on the fund and Zcash's privacy features.

The campaign extends across print, outdoor ads, digital, streaming and social media, Grayscale said in a Tuesday release.

Zcash is getting the full-page treatment in today’s Wall Street Journal. Grayscale has taken out a front-page ad promoting Zcash, along with another full-page ad inside highlighting its Zcash investment fund (ZCSH). https://t.co/6lNtfTaViI

The firm is also expanding grants to nonprofits and initiatives supporting Zcash to $1 million. It has already awarded $400,000 through four equal grants to the Zcash Foundation, policy advocacy group Pretty Good Policy for Zcash, Shielded Labs and Project Tachyon.

Grayscale also said it intends to reinvest "100% of $ZCSH sponsor fees into the Zcash network across development, marketing, community and education" for up to the fund's first year as an ETF.

"Financial privacy is becoming essential as more economic activity moves onchain and AI makes it easier to analyze," Steve Vanourny, Grayscale’s head of index, said in the announcement.

The campaign comes as Zcash's native ZEC trades down roughly 13% on the day to $1,025, close to a two-month low. ZCSH shares are also down around 12% to $27.25, putting them near their lowest level over the same period.

The fund has seen more than $15 million in net outflows so far in November, excluding Tuesday's figures, following $46.25 million in net outflows during October.

The fund began as a private placement in 2017 and listed on NYSE Arca in August.

Bitcoin’s projected difficulty cut would still leave older S19 rigs short at 5-cent power

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

At unchanged Bitcoin price, subsidy and fees, the projected 6.5% difficulty cut would leave modeled 95 and 90 TH/s S19s below electricity coverage at 5 cents/kWh.

Bitcoin miners could earn about 7% more per unit of computing power if a projected difficulty cut materializes, but the relief would leave two older S19 variants needing electricity near 4 cents per kilowatt-hour just to cover machine power.

Hashrate Index’s November 9 snapshot, dated 18:00 UTC, projected a 6.5% difficulty reduction around November 13. The mining data platform put current difficulty at 148.36 trillion and spot hashprice at $31.89 per petahash per second per day. Hashprice measures expected daily revenue from a unit of mining capacity, before expenses.

With Bitcoin’s price, block subsidy and average transaction fees per block unchanged, that projected cut would lift hashprice to about $34.11. It would improve the electricity cushion for miners still operating, while leaving a large gap between older equipment and a more efficient S21.

Lower difficulty increases the probability that a given amount of computing work earns a block reward. Expected revenue per unit of work therefore moves inversely with difficulty when the value of rewards stays fixed.

The scenario calculation divides $31.89 by 0.935, the share of difficulty remaining after a 6.5% reduction. That produces a 7% increase and isolates the retarget’s effect. Actual dollar receipts would also depend on Bitcoin pricing and fees.

Bitcoin recalculates difficulty every 2,016 blocks, using block timestamps to target roughly two weeks. November 13 and the size of the cut remain estimates that can change as more blocks arrive.

The useful operating test is the highest electricity tariff gross mining revenue can cover. Dividing expected daily receipts by the machine’s daily wall-power consumption gives that electricity-only ceiling. A tariff below the ceiling leaves something toward other expenses; a tariff above it means machine electricity alone exceeds gross revenue.

BITMAIN’s S19 specifications list two model 240-Ca variants at 95 and 90 terahashes per second, both drawing a nominal 3,250 watts at 25 degrees Celsius. Its S21 specification lists 200 TH/s at 3,500 watts under the same temperature condition.

The calculations assume continuous operation at nominal ratings and a 6.5% difficulty cut, with Bitcoin price, subsidy and average fees per block unchanged. They exclude pool fees, separate cooling and site loads, repairs, labor, hosting overhead, financing and equipment costs.

A projected 6.5% difficulty cut implies 6.95% more revenue per hash with Bitcoin price, subsidy and average fees per block held constant.

At $31.89/PH/s/day, both illustrative tariff cases fall short of machine electricity coverage.

At the modeled $34.11/PH/s/day, the S19 still falls short at 5¢/kWh; the S21 barely covers electricity at 8¢/kWh.

Under the projected cut, the older S19 stays below its illustrative power bill; the S21 gains only a thin electricity cushion.

November 13 and the 6.5% cut are projections from the Nov. 9, 18:00 UTC snapshot. Price, subsidy and average fees per block remain fixed. Bars use a shared 0–10 cents/kWh scale and unrounded inputs; labels round to two decimals. Nominal 95 TH/s/3250 W S19 and 200 TH/s/3500 W S21 run continuously. Tariffs of 5 and 8 cents/kWh are illustrative.

At an illustrative 5 cents/kWh, both S19 variants would still earn less than their electricity bill after the cut. A nominal S21 paying 8 cents/kWh would move from below to just above electricity coverage, leaving a thin cushion of about 10 cents a day before other costs. The two tariffs are illustrative.

The comparison uses each model’s stated power and hashrate. Actual machines can differ: BITMAIN gives the S19 variants hashrate tolerance of plus or minus 3% and wall-power tolerance of plus or minus 5%. The S21 specification gives no numeric tolerance. Each ceiling applies to the specific model and nominal settings shown.

CryptoSlate’s October 14 analysis modeled the electricity squeeze from a projected difficulty increase, while its November 4 report examined a forward market implying little additional mining competition.

The next test is the completed adjustment and hashprice afterward. At the November 9 inputs, the projected cut would expand electricity coverage. Whether that becomes a viable operating margin depends on the actual revenue rate, equipment performance and costs beyond the machine’s power draw.

Pas tendre Zoumana !

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Allez Paillade, le site non officiel dédié à l'actualité du Montpellier HSC revient sur la surprenante défaite à domicile des Héraultais contre le FC Metz (2-3) et relate les propos du coach Zoumana Camara, pas tendre avec ses joueurs qui ont laissé filer une occasion de rester dans la roue des Verts :

"Je suis déçu. La leçon, c’est qu’un match se joue 90 minutes. On a été absent des débats sur les 45 premières minutes. Et même sur cette deuxième mi-temps où on est revenu avec de meilleures intentions. Quand on met ce 2-1, on ne doit pas encaisser ce troisième but. On a le sentiment d’avoir fait le plus dur, de pouvoir emballer le match et de créer le doute. Et derrière, on prend un troisième but quelques minutes après. Le sentiment que rien ne tourne dans le bon sens dans cette soirée.

L’absence d’agressivité, dans l’intensité, dans le rythme. Quand on ne met pas ces ingrédients-là, on tombe face à une équipe où il y avait un tout. Une équipe qui a pris un sévère revers à la maison, qui vient de perdre dans la semaine son président historique. Et puis un ancien joueur de la maison qui revient. Donc vous mettez tout ça ensemble, ça crée un esprit et une équipe qui est venue en mode commando, en mode combat avec l’envie de faire quelque chose. Et si nous, on n’a pas la capacité à avoir au moins cette même envie, cette même agressivité, cette même détermination avant de parler du jeu, on ne peut pas exister dans ce championnat et on n’a pas existé aujourd’hui, en tout cas sur la première période.

(Un relâchement ?) Peut-être. J’ai des jeunes joueurs, on est en train d’apprendre un peu dans tout. C’est l’avenir qui dira si c’est un accident ou pas. Mais en tout cas, on était absent des débats aujourd’hui, surtout sur la première mi-temps.

(Un coup d’arrêt ?) Une saison, c’est long. C’est très très long. L’environnement était euphorique. Et parfois ultra-enthousiaste. J’ai dû la saison passée venir beaucoup de fois ici. On parlait d’être un peu plus réaliste face à la situation. Aujourd’hui, les résultats faisaient qu’il y avait cette euphorie. Et c’est juste à juste titre. C’est à nous de devoir, à l’intérieur, canaliser ça, garder les pieds sur terre, continuer à avancer et continuer à mettre les ingrédients qui ont fait la réussite. Et là, on ne les a pas mis ce soir."

Cribl Buys Tero to Build Observability for Humans and AI Agents

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Cribl announced on November 10, 2026 that it had acquired Tero, a company whose technology identifies low-value observability data. Tero’s founders and engineers are joining Cribl to develop observability for engineers and AI agents, marking the telemetry company’s third acquisition in four months.

Ben Johnson, Adam Cohen and Nihar Singhal, formerly Tero’s CEO, CTO and COO, will help lead Cribl’s observability roadmap. Johnson becomes VP of Observability, with the team working on host monitoring, application performance monitoring (APM), federated observability and AI-native observability on the platform customers already use to collect, route and optimize telemetry. Johnson previously co-founded Timber, creator of the open-source Vector observability pipeline. Datadog acquired Timber in 2021.

Tero’s technology measures how much of an organization’s telemetry is waste, locates low-value data across observability tools and lets engineers address it within their existing tools. That adds a data-value decision to Cribl’s collection and routing work: teams also need to determine which telemetry merits retention and analysis.

In Johnson’s account of the acquisition, he said flexible routing had not eliminated the cost of retaining and querying telemetry that went unread. Identifying useful data depends on knowing how services connect, what changed and which signals engineers consult during incidents. That knowledge is often undocumented and held by engineers. It can guide a human investigation, but the arrangement breaks down when an AI agent conducts it.

Johnson describes AI agents as emerging primary consumers of production data alongside engineers, with both groups needing the underlying data and the context to interpret it. His example is an agent investigating a latency spike: it must connect the symptom to a deployment from an hour earlier, the code change behind that deployment and a downstream service that started timing out. Separate observability products can restrict the evidence available to the investigation.

The proposed architecture maintains production understanding once as a shared resource for engineers and agents. Host monitoring and APM would continue as applications on that foundation, rather than independent product stores duplicating the same telemetry. Johnson, his co-founders and founding engineer Jacob Aronoff are bringing Tero’s work on agent access and production understanding to Cribl.

In a July 28, 2026 post on agentic telemetry, Cribl CEO Clint Sharp defined the term as telemetry managed for people and AI agents together. The approach combines human, machine and AI context so authorized users can query data where it resides.

Federated search, or search-in-place, brings queries to data in lakes, object storage, lakehouses and existing tools rather than first centralizing the data. Agent access also requires identity, role-based access control, audit, lineage, budget controls and human inspection of queries and actions. “Models will change. Agents will change. Interfaces will change. Your telemetry is the durable asset,” Sharp wrote.

The deal follows CardinalOps in July 2026 and Radiant Security’s AI security operations center technology in August 2026. Cribl says these acquisitions extend its business toward first-party observability and security capabilities on one open telemetry platform.

For Cribl, Tero adds expertise in data value, production context and agent access, while the planned monitoring work would put more first-party applications on that shared foundation. The central test is whether Cribl can give authorized agents broad access to usable production evidence without recreating the isolated data stores and ungoverned access its architecture is designed to avoid.

XRP Ledger Unveils Fee-Free Quantum-Proof Upgrade as Ripple Races Toward 2028 Security Deadline: Here’s What’s Coming

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The XRP Ledger is preparing for a major security overhaul as quantum computing moves from a theoretical concern to a growing priority for the crypto industry. With Ripple targeting 2028 for its post-quantum security transition, developers are now unveiling a proposed quantum-resistant upgrade that promises to protect users against future attacks without introducing additional transaction fees.

The development could mark a significant turning point for the network, raising questions about what exactly is changing, how the upgrade will work, and whether XRP holders will need to do anything before the looming security deadline.

The XRP community has a new development to watch as the XRP Ledger Foundation outlines plans to strengthen the network against future quantum computing risks. The proposed upgrade would introduce quantum-resistant accounts, giving users an additional layer of protection against emerging cryptographic threats while keeping transaction costs unchanged.

Although the feature has yet to be implemented, the proposed upgrade would allow XRP Ledger users to register NIST-approved ML-DSA-44 quantum-resistant signing keys and deactivate their existing authentication keys. This would give accounts an additional layer of protection against potential quantum computing attacks, particularly those whose public keys have already been exposed on the network.

Importantly, users could adopt the enhanced security system without changing how they store, hold, or access their XRP. The concern stems from how transactions are authorized on the network. When users sign transactions through their cryptocurrency wallets, their public keys can become exposed, potentially creating an attack vector if sufficiently advanced quantum computers emerge.

Such technology could eventually undermine existing cryptographic protections, putting accounts with exposed public keys at risk. The XRP Ledger Foundation’s proposed solution is designed to address this vulnerability before quantum computing advances to the point where such attacks become practically achievable.

Beyond the additional protection, the proposal has another notable selling point: affordability. Users would not have to pay higher transaction fees to benefit from the enhanced security. In other words, XRP holders could strengthen their accounts against emerging quantum threats without incurring additional transaction costs, making the proposed upgrade particularly appealing to the community.

Although the upgrade has not yet gone live, the team has outlined its next steps, with a migration study planned for the first half of 2027. The research will explore how existing XRPL accounts can move to the new quantum-resistant security framework, including the transition process and what account holders would need to do to adopt the upgraded protection.

Developers are also addressing discrepancies in how the proposed system generates cryptographic keys across its Python, JavaScript, and server implementations, a necessary step toward ensuring consistent and reliable performance across the network.

As ZyCrypto previously reported, Ripple has outlined a comprehensive four-phase roadmap to make the XRP Ledger, its decentralized Layer-1 blockchain, resistant to potential quantum computing attacks, with full quantum readiness targeted for 2028.

L'attaquant belge est arrivé à Nervión en prêt de Saint-Étienne avec pour mission de dynamiser l'attaque de l'équipe entraînée par Luis García Plaza. Cependant, au fil des semaines, les attentes placées dans le jeune attaquant se sont estompées, puisqu'il traverse actuellement une période de disette. Depuis son but en septembre face à l’Espanyol Barcelone, l'attaquant belge n'a pas trouvé le chemin des filets et peine à se montrer suffisamment présent dans le dernier tiers du terrain pour menacer régulièrement les défenses adverses. Mauricio Pochettino interested in leaving USMNT job for Manchester United

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Coach’s contract has release clause of about $10m

United expected to sack Michael Carrick after awful start

US men’s national team head coach Mauricio Pochettino is interested in a move to Manchester United, multiple sources told the Guardian, with those same sources characterizing the interest as mutual.

United are expected to sack their head coach, Michael Carrick, after a disastrous start to their season which leaves them at risk of relegation from the Premier League, their worst opening to a campaign in four decades.

Securing Pochettino’s services is far from simple or inexpensive. The Argentinian’s contract contains a release clause in the neighborhood of $10m (£7.5m), multiple sources familiar with the deal told the Guardian this week, a figure that grows should United choose to retain Pochettino’s staff, including his longtime assistant and confidant Jesús Pérez, who has worked with Pochettino for 16 years. The Athletic first reported the details of Pochettino’s buyout.

Pochettino was brought on by the US in 2024 and guided the USMNT to a last-16 finish at the 2026 World Cup, by most accounts a disappointment. US Soccer brought him back through 2030, with both sides expressing a mutual belief and commitment towards not only brightening the fortunes of the US men but also overhauling the federation’s approach towards youth development.

“Every time we sign a contract, it’s to go to the last day of the contract,” Pochettino told reporters after his contract renewal. “If we sign a four-year contract, we need to think to arrive there. And we signed the contract because we really believe that we are going to be four years there. Because if not, it’s bad. If we don’t believe in that process, if we don’t believe in that journey, why sign [for] four years? Only to be sure that we are going to receive a salary at the end of the month? That is not nice. It’s not the way that we think. We really signed that contract because we really believe that we can arrive to the end.”

Pochettino, who has been linked with a host of high-profile club openings since his arrival in the United States, tempered those remarks by adding another: “In life, nothing is sure. Less in football.”

The USMNT are days from a pair of Concacaf Nations League matches against Haiti in Cincinnati and Miami, their first competitive fixtures of the 2030 qualifying cycle. Pochettino is scheduled to address reporters on Friday afternoon.

Pochettino has repeatedly expressed his interest in returning to the Premier League during his time with the US. As recently as September, he spoke to Sky Sports about the idea of winning the league and “building a project with the possibility of winning”. In late 2025 he said much the same, telling the BBC he believed the league was the best in the world and adding “of course I am missing it. I am so happy in America but am thinking but also thinking one day to come back.”

Beyond his stated interest, Pochettino is also widely known as a coach who enjoys the day-to-day responsibilities of coaching and he’s widely regarded as an excellent man-manager who thrives in the daily rhythm of life at a club. He maintains a home in London and speaks glowingly of the UK and the football culture that permeates it. In short, there are no shortage of reasons for him to leave the US.

Most federations have contingency plans developed for the departure of a head coach and the US federation is no exception, especially given the repeated interest in Pochettino over the years. Prior to bringing the Argentine on board, US Soccer reached out to or spoke to several other high-profile candidates, most notably former Liverpool and current Germany boss Jürgen Klopp.

Jonathan Wilson and Guardian journalists bring expert analysis and reporting on the biggest stories in soccer.

In the nearer term, several domestic candidates could fit the bill – current U-23 head coach Steve Cherundolo being one. Nashville SC head coach (and former US interim head coach) BJ Callaghan could be another candidate close to home, having guided the MLS club to a league-record points total this season.

In some respects, there is no better time for Pochettino to depart; doing so now would allow an incoming coach the majority of a four-year cycle to work with and the federation itself would earn millions just months after Pochettino could’ve departed for free.

That feels highly unlikely. Pochettino enjoys an unusually close relationship with his assistants, in particular his second-in-command, Jesús Pérez. Pérez has served alongside Pochettino at every stop in his coaching career, from La Liga side Espanyol in 2010 to stops at Southampton, Tottenham Hotspur, PSG and Chelsea in the 15 years that followed. It’s rare to see Pochettino anywhere in a professional capacity without Pérez at his side.

Pochettino has similarly close bonds with his other assistants – Miki D’Agostino, goalkeeper coach Toni Jiménez and several others. They, too, have been with the Argentine since the start.

Repubblica: Milan and Real Madrid watching as Napoli star McTominay stalls on contract

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Scott McTominay’s Napoli future is in doubt, and a report has named AC Milan among the surprise potential suitors.

In a Napoli side that appears to have lost its way, one of the few lights leading the way remains McTominay. Despite a season that started on the wrong foot and a heart condition (now resolved), the Scot has five goals in 11 games this season across Serie A and the Champions League.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Massimiliano Allegri’s team is going through a tough time, and the former Manchester United player himself has had some tough nights. As La Repubblica (via Calciomercato.com) report, Napoli fans are beginning to worry about where things are heading.

The 29-year-old McTominay is at a turning point in his career, one which has been revitalised since his arrival at Napoli and the Scudetto won under Antonio Conte. His current contract with the Neapolitans expires in 2028, but negotiations for a renewal have stalled.

Fabrizio Romano reported that Napoli remain confident in the possibility of reaching an agreement over an extension, but the uncertainty currently surrounding the project, the bad results and doubts about the club’s future are forcing him to evaluate.

La Repubblica add that the discussions have even stalled after positive steps were taken in September. McTominay and his representatives want to better understand the Azzurri’s vision moving forward before finalising a new agreement.

Sky Switzerland journalist Sacho Tavolieri reported that the former Manchester United man’s agent has already held discussions with several English clubs. Napoli have not completely ruled out a possible sale, and the figure would be €25-30m.

His contract is currently worth €4.5m net per season. Napoli would like to extend it until 2030 with a salary increase, but they are unwilling to accept the Scotsman’s demands, who has requested around €7-8m net per season.

According to La Repubblica, at the end of the season McTominay and Napoli will sit down to discuss the terms of the new contract, once the club’s plans are clearer. Several clubs are interested though and are watching closely.

Aston Villa and Manchester United enquired in the summer. In the coming weeks, Real Madrid and AC Milan will also be keeping a close eye on the developments surrounding McTominay.

The Rossoneri are currently well-stocked in midfield with Luka Modric, Adrien Rabiot, Yunus Musah, Ardon Jashari plus options like Ruben Loftus-Cheek and the young Christian Comotto. However, with Amorim at the helm, the prospect of profound change is never far away.

Stankovic leaves Serbia camp and returns to Inter with adductor injury

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Aleksandar Stankovic is the latest Inter player to withdraw from their national team camp with an injury issue, as the Serbian FA has confirmed that he has withdrawn after picking up an adductor injury while in training on Monday.

Stankovic was one of five Serie A players originally called up to the Serbia national team squad last week, along with his brother Filip, Napoli goalkeeper Vanja Milinkovic-Savic, Atalanta midfielder Lazar Samardzic and Milan defender Strahinja Pavlovic, who has also withdrawn through injury.

The Serbian FA has confirmed in a statement on Tuesday that Stankovic has withdrawn from the squad and that he will not be with the team for their upcoming Nations League matches against Germany and Greece.

The Inter midfielder trained with the Serbia squad on Monday, but reported pain in his right groin and has since reported for medical tests, which have revealed a muscular injury in his right thigh.

Stankovic will return to Milan for further treatment with Inter.

Fellow Nerazzurri internationals Ange-Yoan Bonny and Marcus Thuram have also withdrawn from the Ivory Coast and France squads respectively with injury complaints over the last two days. Bonny is reportedly set to miss around a month’s worth of action.

Crypto News Today: Is Bitcoin Price Heading To $69,000?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Bitcoin has dropped to its lowest level in 82 days, trading near $72,936. It is down 3.5% in 24 hours and 6.2% over the past week. Amid the price action, one detail is worrying traders: the amount of leveraged money betting on Bitcoin keeps rising even as the price falls.

Open interest, the total value of open futures positions, has climbed steadily since early October while Bitcoin has trended lower.

That is unusual. When prices fall, leveraged traders usually get forced out and open interest drops. This time the opposite is happening. When those positions are eventually closed or liquidated, the result could be “a huge volatility event.”

The latest leg lower came after a hotter-than-expected US inflation report raised the odds of a December Fed rate hike. Expert Michaël van de Poppe said Bitcoin’s reaction to the report was “fairly good” but that the bigger picture has not changed. Bitcoin is still in a downtrend and needs to win back a key price level before turning bullish.

Van de Poppe also sees early signs of life in altcoins, which have started to move differently from Bitcoin. In his view, that split could lead to “a strong surge” in altcoins once Bitcoin finally bottoms.

Bitcoin has slipped below the $74,000 to $76,000 support zone that had held for weeks. Attention is now on lower levels:

A new bullish divergence is forming on the 12-hour chart. Bitcoin has made a lower low in price while momentum, measured by the RSI, may be making a higher low. That often comes before a short-term bounce.

If it confirms, the next upside target is $76,500 to $77,000, where a cluster of liquidation orders is building. The last time this signal appeared, in mid-October, Bitcoin saw roughly a week of modest relief before falling again.

On longer timeframes, the outlook is mixed:

A stronger recovery in US stocks could help Bitcoin. Without one, any bounce may be short-lived.

Lumbra Raises $25M Series A to Expand Controlled AI Agent Deployments

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Lumbra announced on November 10, 2026 that it raised a $25 million Series A co-led by Point72 Ventures and Ansa, with continued participation from existing investor Costanoa, to expand its controlled AI-agent platform beyond national security into regulated commercial sectors.

Most of the proceeds will fund engineers and deployment teams pursuing customers in finance, healthcare, law and other regulated industries. Lumbra is also hiring across product and go-to-market roles, including new graduates. The company says its agents already operate in production on top-secret networks across the Pentagon and U.S. Intelligence Community.

LumbraOS is model-neutral, allowing customers to combine frontier-lab models with open-source alternatives. The platform has three components: Nebula manages agent execution, StarChart holds organizational knowledge, and Astrolabe handles evaluation. Nebula links individual claims to their sources, records what agents do and applies data protections. Domain experts must validate results before an agent can write them back to a system of record.

StarChart’s knowledge graph stores insights and conclusions alongside their sources and sensitivity labels. Customers retain the data rights and can export their graphs. Astrolabe evaluates agents ahead of rollout, then continues scoring live systems against custom benchmarks. Each agent accesses information where it is stored and under that source’s permissions, so customer data does not need to be duplicated.

Lumbra’s technology documentation describes evaluation as a continuous process, with every result traceable through its reasoning, underlying documents and the checks it passed. Validation compares outputs with reference material that people specify and maintain, instead of depending only on another model to judge the output. The orchestration around stochastic models is designed to be deterministic, giving oversight teams a repeatable execution path whenever the workflow, context and evaluation criteria are unchanged. The platform supports air-gapped, on-premises and sovereign-cloud deployments, with no data leaving the boundary, audited dependencies and signed, reproducible container images.

Co-founder and CEO Aaron Brown described the intended change in how operators supervise agents: “The organizations that get agents right will spend less time telling them what to do and more time deciding what they may touch and what their work must prove.”

Lumbra reports two early government milestones alongside the round: it signed its first U.S. intelligence-agency contract two months after product work began and moved a later Department of War contract from award to production on top-secret networks in six months.

The Department of War launched Agent Network on June 25, 2026, naming Palantir Technologies and Lumbra in its official release as contributors to a project led by the Chief Digital and Artificial Intelligence Office with U.S. Pacific, Southern and European Commands. The system uses agents to examine defense intelligence and operational systems, turning findings into options for commanders. Target selection and strikes remain human decisions; the system does not take either action autonomously. Testing, operational evaluation and oversight will continue during development and fielding.

Commercial work is already at the engagement stage, according to Lumbra’s domains documentation. Financial-data providers and research teams are working with the company on diligence, sanctions screening and supply-chain risk. Target workflows also include financial research and know-your-customer and anti-money-laundering checks. The clinical work includes active engagements and live surgical-preparation workflows, with revenue-cycle and population-health uses identified as adjacent applications.

The Series A therefore connects Lumbra’s commercial ambitions to a specific test: whether controls built for classified networks can meet the governance demands of regulated buyers while the company adds the engineering and deployment staff needed to support them.

Idaho Man Sentenced to 18 Years in Prison for Attempting to Provide Material Support to ISIS in Plot to Attack Churches

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

An official website of the United States government

Official websites use .gov A .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPS A lock ( Lock Locked padlock ) or https:// means you’ve safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

Alexander Scott Mercurio, 21, of Coeur d’Alene, Idaho, was sentenced today to 18 years in prison and lifetime supervised release by U.S. District Judge David C. Nye of the District of Idaho for attempting to provide material support and resources to a designated foreign terrorist organization.

According to court records, Mercurio planned to incapacitate his father with a metal pipe and take his firearms to use in a mass casualty attack on Idaho churches in the name of ISIS. Mercurio also planned to use flame-covered weapons, explosives, knives, and a machete in the attacks. A search of his house revealed that he had butane canisters, lighters, a pair of handcuffs, a knife, and a machete, as well as several firearms belonging to his father. Days before the planned attack, Mercurio stood before an ISIS flag holding a knife and pledged his allegiance to ISIS. The FBI arrested him on April 6, 2024, one day before his planned attack. In addition to his plot to attack Idaho churches, in early 2023, he created an online repository for pro-ISIS content with the purpose of spreading the doctrine of the Islamic State.

“America’s founders wanted our Nation to be a place where the faithful of any denomination could exercise their religious beliefs freely and without fear of violence,” said Principal Deputy Assistant Attorney General for National Security M. Scott Proctor. “Alexander Scott Mercurio planned to attack that fundamental freedom by bringing death and destruction to several churches. This sentence shows that the Department of Justice will relentlessly investigate, pursue, and prosecute those who would terrorize and murder Americans for exercising their religious rights.”

“The sentencing of Mercurio today sends the clear message that where individuals intend to pursue acts of terrorism against the United States – whether in Coeur d’Alene, or any other community – they will be brought to justice,” said U.S. Attorney John C. Shirts for the District of Idaho. “Protecting our communities from terrorist activity is always the number one priority of this office and the Department of Justice. I commend the investigation by the FBI, as well as the valuable assistance provided by the Coeur d’Alene Police Department, the Kootenai County Sheriff’s Office and the Ada County Sheriff’s Office.”

“With today’s sentencing, this individual is being held accountable for pledging allegiance to ISIS and planning an attack on U.S. soil,” said Assistant Director Jarod Brown of the FBI’s Counterterrorism Division. “The FBI works tirelessly to detect and prevent acts of terrorism in order to protect the American people. We will continue to work closely with our law enforcement and Justice Department partners to make sure anyone who participates in terrorist acts faces justice.”

The case was investigated by the FBI’s Joint Terrorism Task Force with assistance from the Coeur d’Alene Police Department, the Kootenai County Sheriff’s Office and the Ada County Sheriff’s Office.

The case was prosecuted by Assistant U.S. Attorneys Heather S. Patricco and David G. Robins for the District of Idaho and Trial Attorney Matthew R. Hracho of the National Security Division’s Counterterrorism Section.

The indie game community is in disarray over AI and Big Walk

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Big Walk’s developers said that they used AI ‘sparingly’ to help work through ‘difficult technical challenges.’

Big Walk’s developers said that they used AI ‘sparingly’ to help work through ‘difficult technical challenges.’

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

If you buy something from a link, The Verge may earn a commission. See our ethics statement.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Some Independent Games Festival (IGF) jurors published a statement yesterday saying they would not vote for games that use generative AI in “any capacity.” Soon after, the developer of indie hit Big Walk has published a statement saying it “sparingly” used AI in the “final stretch” of the game’s development to “help work through difficult technical challenges and bugs.”

According to the jurors’ statement, a copy of which was posted by Austin Walker, while this year’s IGF entries have been “incredible,” there has been “a significant increase” in the number of games “developed with generative AI tools,” leading to “fairly intense discussion and disagreement” with no “universal consensus.” The statement includes a list of beliefs, including that generative AI has “negative effects,” that AI-generated code is “not an exception to these harms,” and that use of AI tools is “not a forgone conclusion in our industry.”

IGF jurors that signed include Telling Lies and Immortality creator Sam Barlow and Celeste composer Lena Raine. The jurors say that the statement doesn’t speak for IGF as an organization or for all IGF jury members, noting that some voiced concern about being able to safely sign “without professional blowback.”

One contentious title was apparently Big Walk, the co-op adventure game from Untitled Goose Game developer House House. In a statement to Aftermath and posted on its website, House House said that “In some cases, snippets from LLM responses were copied as a starting point to be modified into game code—we’d estimate that well under 0.1% of Big Walk’s code had anything to do with this usage, and generative AI was not used in any other context when making the game.”

House House said its “primary use case” of LLMs was “to research, via chatbots, issues we encountered with third-party code,” including fixing an issue with shadows by modifying LLM-suggested code. House House noted it turned to the “LLM chatbot” after researching the problem “extensively” and even speaking to an engineer at Unity.

The studio said it disclosed its AI usage as part of its IGF submission, though it has not disclosed AI usage on Big Walk’s Steam page because “the platform does not request disclosure of code or chatbot usage.”

“This is not an endorsement of, or argument for, the use of AI tools, however minimal,” House House said. “We have some concern that voluntary disclosures like this might contribute to the normalisation of this technology, or its treatment as ‘inevitable’. We support the work of those who are trying to limit the harm generative AI is doing to videogames and our world, and apologise that Big Walk’s LLM usage may not meet their standards.”

House House didn’t have an AI policy in place when it developed Big Walk, which it described as a “failure,” and said it will “work to have a more comprehensive policy going forwards.”

Barlow said after we published this post that Big Walk was not the “impetus” for the IGF’s discussion, but it became the “tricky edge case — well what about this minimal use of AI, what about when your favorite game, or team uses it?” Barlow added that “This is not a call to boycott a certain game, or to economically hurt a team — this was about a single award’s show of many and what that award should mean to reflect our ‘indie’ alternative to mainstream and larger scale game development.” Walker also shared a long blog post that’s worth reading about his position on AI and the IGF awards.

Update, November 10th: Added posts from Barlow and Walker.

IBM and Microsoft Launch Imagine Enterprise for Industry AI Agents

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

IBM Consulting and Microsoft announced Imagine Enterprise on November 10, 2026, a jointly developed platform for creating, operating and overseeing AI agents tailored to industry processes, drawing on IBM Consulting’s inventory of more than 6,000 pre-built AI agents and assets.

The platform pairs IBM’s industry and delivery expertise with Microsoft’s AI, data, cloud, identity and security technologies. It is designed to connect with an enterprise’s existing data, processes and systems, with the aim of letting organizations adapt pre-built capabilities without replacing their current technology environment or assembling each component themselves.

Forward-deployed engineers from both companies can work with customer teams on qualifying engagements to help move projects from experimentation into production. Clients can engage IBM Consulting to deploy and operate the platform, collaborate with IBM on enterprise-scale implementations, or choose self-management.

Initial industry frameworks cover financial services, insurance and retail. Manufacturing, energy, the public sector and mobility are identified only as possible future additions.

IBM’s launch overview says the companies jointly invested in and co-developed the platform. IBM Consulting Advantage’s Process Studio and Context Studio connect agents to an organization’s own data and processes, while Microsoft Foundry, GitHub Copilot and Copilot Studio help coordinate agents with enterprise systems. Microsoft Fabric, Microsoft 365, Entra, Purview, Defender and Azure provide the surrounding data, identity, governance, security and cloud foundation.

That product division places governance inside the deployment stack rather than leaving it to a separate oversight exercise. For regulated industries, the platform includes controls, observability and AI-compliance support intended to govern agents connected to enterprise knowledge and daily work.

IBM says every agent action, decision and escalation is logged, with real-time data on latency, cost, token usage and run history. Governance controls set which actions an agent may take autonomously, which require human approval and when escalation is mandatory, and agents cannot bypass their defined authority limits.

An operational intelligence layer monitors performance and recommends improvements, with approved changes redeployed automatically. Microsoft Defender, Purview, Entra and Sentinel add real-time visibility over agents in production. The operating model therefore leaves defined approval decisions with people while automating performance monitoring and the redeployment of approved changes.

IBM’s example applies the model to insurance claims, a document-intensive process that runs from first notification of loss through settlement. Based on client interviews, standard operating procedures and industry benchmarks, the process is divided among digital workers with defined responsibilities, controls and escalation paths.

A Digital Steward orchestrates the work. It assesses claim complexity, flags possible fraud signals, checks policy coverage and routes cases according to their characteristics and an adjuster’s authority. Simple claims can go to junior adjusters, coverage ambiguity to senior adjusters, and complex cases with multiple anomaly flags to principal adjusters or specialist investigation units.

The employee receives a prioritized queue explaining why work was routed, what has already been investigated and the recommended next action. The example leaves the claim decision with the human; digital workers handle preparation, triage and follow-through.

SEI, a provider of financial technology, operations and asset-management services, is exploring emerging and agentic technologies with IBM Consulting and Microsoft support. It is also using other technologies from both companies to improve its operations and the experiences it delivers to employees and clients. Its role in the announcement is exploratory, without quantified production results for Imagine Enterprise.

Sneha Shah, SEI’s Chief AI Strategist and Head of SEI Next, said: “Our work with IBM Consulting and Microsoft is helping us explore how emerging capabilities could support critical processes and be responsibly scaled over time, with the goal to create better experiences for our employees and clients.”

The launch connects a large consulting asset base to an extensive enterprise technology stack, but its adoption model depends on more than supplying agents. The initial industry frameworks, approval boundaries and deployment options define a specific execution test: turning pre-built components into governed operations that fit the way individual enterprises already work.

Birmingham City, West Brom & West Ham will lick their lips at major Crystal Palace update

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

As we head into the November international break, it is shaping up to be a competitive battle for automatic promotion in the Championship.

With just six points separating the teams currently sitting in first and ninth place, it remains incredibly tight towards the top of the Championship, but events over the next fortnight could have a big impact on the promotion race.

Birmingham City hit the top of the Championship table after they recorded their third consecutive victory with a 2-1 win over Swansea City on Saturday, and Chris Davies' men look to be building strong momentum.

The Blues replaced Middlesbrough at the summit on goal difference after a disappointing week for the Teesside outfit, who followed up their midweek defeat at Wrexham with an underwhelming 0-0 draw at Watford.

Surprise package Stoke City are level on points with Birmingham and Boro after their impressive run continued with a 2-1 victory at Portsmouth, while Midlands duo Wolverhampton Wanderers and West Bromwich Albion are just a point behind the trio.

Swansea, Southampton and Queens Park Rangers complete the play-off places, with West Ham United dropping out of the top eight after their turbulent start to life under new head coach Scott Parker continued with a 2-1 defeat at Blackburn Rovers.

While the aforementioned nine teams appear to be the most likely automatic promotion contenders as things stand, it is difficult to ignore a resurgent Norwich City, who recorded their fourth consecutive win as they hammered Bristol City 5-0 to move within three points of the play-offs.

The international break will give many of the promotion hopefuls a chance to rest after a busy period, but recent developments at Crystal Palace may have Middlesbrough and Swansea feeling a little uneasy.

There are yet to be any managerial sackings in the Premier League this season, but that appears certain to change during the international break as Pierre Sage's future at Crystal Palace hangs in the balance.

Sage only took over at Selhurst Park in the summer when he replaced Oliver Glasner, but the Eagles have won just two of their opening 10 league games, and they currently sit bottom of the table, two points from safety.

Palace's poor start continued as they were beaten 3-1 at home by Liverpool on Sunday, and, after weeks of speculation over Sage's position, it was reported that the club were preparing to part company with the Frenchman.

It seems the Eagles have already drawn up a shortlist of potential replacements for Sage, with Middlesbrough's Kim Hellberg and Swansea's Vitor Matos said to be two of the names under consideration, along with Thomas Frank, Frank Lampard, Sean Dyche, Vitor Pereira, Tony Popovic, Jens Berthel Askou and Ole Werner.

Former Brentford and Tottenham Hotspur boss Frank is reportedly a leading contender to succeed Sage, which may offer some reassurance to Middlesbrough and Swansea, but news that Hellberg and Matos are attracting top flight interest will be music to the ears of their promotion rivals.

Given that Middlesbrough stole Hellberg from under Swansea's noses last November before the Welsh side turned to Matos, there is a certain irony to the fact that the pair are now both on Crystal Palace's radar.

While Hellberg inherited a strong foundation at the Riverside Stadium after the work of his predecessor, Rob Edwards, the Swede has taken Boro to another level over the past year, building one of the most dangerous attacking sides in the division, as well as implementing an attractive brand of football.

After an 11th-placed finish last season, the Swans have emerged as a surprise promotion contender this time around under Matos, and they led the Championship during the early weeks of the campaign before a recent run of four defeats in five games saw them drop down to sixth.

There is no doubt that Hellberg and Matos have been integral to the success of their respective clubs this season, and it would be a devastating blow for Middlesbrough and Swansea were either to lose their highly rated coaches, while simultaneously providing a huge boost to the likes of Birmingham, Wolves, West Brom and West Ham.

Given that Boro have assembled one of the strongest squads in the Championship after their summer spending spree, it seems unlikely that Hellberg would be tempted to make the move to Selhurst Park, but Matos could consider jumping ship if the Eagles come calling.

Of course, a blip is to be expected at some stage of the season, but there are question marks over whether the Swans can maintain their promotion push after their recent decline in form, so Matos may be keen to take the opportunity to manage in the Premier League if it were presented to him.

8 Reasons GTA VI Will Be Remembered For Years To Come

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Aden Carter is a Contributor at DualShockers who has been covering games professionally since 2020. He specializes in lists and guides, with a focus on RPGs, horror games, survival horror, Soulslikes, cozy/sim games, and action-adventure titles. Before joining DualShockers, Aden contributed to GameRant, XDA, Gamepur, I

There are games that come once in a decade and define an era for many. There’s a reason why we still talk about titles like The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time or Grand Theft Auto V, for instance. Both games have made such an impact on the gaming community that they will be remembered forever.

The next game to get remembered for years to come is going to be Grand Theft Auto VI. It has already had such hype built up around it that no matter what happens on the release date, we will be talking about it for years.

Of course, there are several other reasons GTA VI will be remembered, whether it is the level of detail, the time put into it, or even the impact on the economy.

Grand Theft Auto V is remembered for its great story, the attention to detail, and many other things. Red Dead Redemption 2 is remembered for these as well. GTA VI will also be remembered for its attention to detail and how it revolutionized open world gaming.

The next Grand Theft Auto title is set to expand the map, increase the level of detail in NPCs, and showcase a level of attention never seen in a game before. If everything goes smoothly at launch, gamers will be talking about how it is the new standard for open world games.

Every game after this will feel like it doesn’t measure up to the detail of Rockstar’s latest project, and it will be used as the benchmark for nearly every other triple-A studio.

Rockstar's 38 new GTA 6 screenshots showcase incredibly detailed faces, Vice City's nightlife, and the gangs and scenes bringing Leonida to life.

One thing that gamers seem to always bring up is how long they waited for any particular game. We finally got a remake of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time, and some of the first things people said was that they waited almost 30 years for it.

Kingdom Hearts players are the same way, as they often mention the wait between Kingdom Hearts 2 and Kingdom Hearts 3. Whenever there is a long wait for a sequel, we tend to latch onto it and bring it up in conversation.

With there being a significant wait of 13 years between GTA V and GTA VI, gamers are going to be talking about it for years to come, bringing it up like they had to wait in line for their favorite game for all those years or something.

Meme culture is powerful and stays around for a long time. For instance, the show King of the Hill stuck around thanks to meme culture. The same can be said for Skyrim, as it has many dedicated memes that have never gone out of style.

Odds are, there will be moments in GTA VI that get memed, whether it is the characters giving an odd look or one of the many wild NPCs. There are already memes using parts of the footage we have already seen, I can only imagine what will come from the game’s full release.

On top of this, the game has already generated the meme of “We got this before GTA VI.” This will evolve to GTA VII, and people will talk about how it used to be GTA VI.

Development studios are always trying to figure out when the best time to launch a game is. Typically, a studio wants to avoid launching a game at the same time as a competitor if the games are too similar. After all, they want to maximize their profits, so it only makes sense.

GTA VI had a massive impact on release schedules. Before anyone even knew the release date of GTA VI, companies were already trying to avoid being caught in the release window. Some were even trying to wait out giving a release date until GTA VI’s release date was announced.

Everyone knew the sway the game would have on the wallets of gamers, and they wanted to avoid potential losses. With this news hitting the mainstream, it will be remembered for a long time.

There’s often a conversation that people have when it comes to games and the consoles they are played on. It's all about what games are console sellers. This is typically reserved for games that are console exclusives, like Demon’s Souls for the PS5 or Gears of War E-Day for the Xbox Series X|S.

However, there has been a trend of people buying up consoles just so they can play GTA VI. They avoided buying a PS5 or an Xbox Series X|S until now just so they can play this game.

That is something that many people won’t forget. No matter if GTA VI is great or it flops, everyone who bought a console for it will remember the game for years to come. You don’t forget why you bought a console. I bought my PS5 specifically for Demon’s Souls.

Okay, GTA Online 2.0, or whatever they want to call it, isn’t going to last forever, but it will last for a long time. After all, GTA V Online has lasted for many years, and people are still playing it despite the game coming out in 2013.

Thanks to constant updates and a dedicated community, the online mode has stayed relevant for years. Now GTA VI Online will take the spotlight, and it will keep everyone’s attention after they finish the story.

Nobody knows when GTA VII will be released, and so GTA VI Online will be the go-to online mode for the Grand Theft Auto experience for possibly a decade or more. That will keep people talking for sure.

GTA Online 2.0 will be coming eventually, and there are some changes that Rockstar should make.

You may have heard it already. “GTA VI will cost the US economy $1 billion.” While that likely isn’t true, it is a game that will have a cultural impact, and there are estimates that state the US economy will take a hit to its productivity when it is released.

There is no telling how much money the economy could potentially lose on November 19th, but some businesses have already stated that they anticipate their workflow to slow down that day, and there are many who have already said they’re going to call out to play GTA VI.

Even if there is a small hit to the US economy, that will still be remembered for years. It will be a day that shows how many gamers became united by one game.

Let’s talk about sales numbers for a moment, because the gaming community remembers when a game does amazingly well and when it falls incredibly short. GTA VI is already doing amazingly well with pre-orders.

It is being estimated that pre-orders for GTA VI have already generated $751 million, and over 7 million units have been pre-ordered. That is a higher revenue than many other triple-A titles make, and that is also only on console, as the game won’t be on PC for a while.

It is estimated that GTA VI will generate over $1 billion in revenue for Rockstar, making it close to being one of the highest grossing games of all time. Give it a few years and sales of the game will most likely surpass even GTA V, which sold over 230 million copies. That is a number people will remember.

From DMA Design to GTA III, GTA V, and RDR2, let's take a look back at how Rockstar Games changed open-world gaming and why GTA 6 has so much to live up to.

A350-1000ULR Sets New Distance Record

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Airbus’ A350-1000ULR has been officially recognized for breaking the Guinness World Record for the longest flight by distance by a commercial aircraft.

The record-breaking flight from Melbourne to Toulouse, France, took place in July and was widely reported on, but was only recently confirmed by Guinness using data validated by the International Aeronautical Federation and flightradar24.

The nonstop journey covered 12,503 nautical miles and lasted 24 hours and 24 minutes. The -1000ULR flew over Sydney and the Pacific on a north-easterly routing toward Los Angeles, crossed North America and the Atlantic, and passed over London on its way to Toulouse. According to Airbus, the 13 people on board witnessed two sunsets and two sunrises.

The crew included three Airbus flight test pilots, two pilots from Qantas, and five Airbus flight test engineers. Also on board were two Airbus design engineers and a representative from the IAF.

Airbus is developing the -1000ULR for Qantas’ Project Sunrise, a yearslong effort to connect the east coast of Australia with far-flung destinations such as New York and London. The manufacturer expects to deliver the first of the type in April 2027. Qantas is aiming to operate the first Project Sunrise flight, from Sydney to London, later that year.

The Australian flag carrier has ordered 12 -1000ULRs.

“In responding to the challenge laid down by Qantas, our teams came together to extend the range of the A350-1000 in an efficient and economically viable way to undertake the world’s longest ever commercial flights,” Lars Wagner, CEO of commercial aircraft at Airbus, said in a news release. “Flying 12,503 nautical miles non-stop, we have fully proven the aircraft’s capability to fulfill its mission. And the world record was the icing on the cake.”

Big Walk Set to Miss Out on Indie Awards Over AI Code Controversy

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Daniel is a lifelong gamer with a love for various genres and gameplay styles who enjoys exploring games and playing from various angles.

In March 2027, we’ll be seeing the next iteration of the Independent Games Festival, a celebration and awards show highlighting some of the biggest achievements in the indie gaming sphere. As far as indie games this past year go, an obvious contender is House House’s Big Walk, a cozy exploration game that encourages groups of users to traverse a large island and solve various puzzles using only basic coordination and communication skills. Big Walk did very well for itself, and was part of the discussion for the IGF awards, but a recent disclosure has put a rather sizable damper on those hopes.

Yesterday, a coalition of judges and jurists for the IGF, represented by game journalist and developer Austin Walker, publicly announced their stance on games made with AI: specifically, after lengthy discussion, the judges decided to refrain from voting for any game that utilized AI in any capacity whatsoever. Walker and the judges’ statement, which was posted to his Bluesky account, didn’t specifically mention Big Walk or House House, but it seems it didn’t take long for onlookers to put the pieces together.

Following intense speculation, one of House House’s developers, Bruno Dias, confirmed on his own Bluesky account that, yes, Big Walk does have a small portion of AI-written code within it that was not originally disclosed to players, though it was disclosed to the IGF judges. As a result of this, there is a distinct chance that any awards Big Walk was previously under consideration for are now forfeit.

After overwhelmingly negative reviews from fans Creative Assembly has responded to the mistakes made in Total War: SHOGUN 2 Complete Edition.

Around the same time as the judges’ announcement and Dias’s Bluesky post, House House updated the official FAQ on its website with information about the usage of AI in Big Walk.

“In the final stretch of Big Walk’s development, LLMs were used sparingly by House House to help work through difficult technical challenges and bugs,” the FAQ reads, estimating that “well under 0.1% of Big Walk’s code had anything to do with this usage, and generative AI was not used in any other context when making the game.”

The FAQ also adds that this statement is not “an endorsement of, or argument for, the use of AI tools, however minimal.”

“We support the work of those who are trying to limit the harm generative AI is doing to videogames and our world, and apologise that Big Walk’s LLM usage may not meet their standards.”

Activision has updated Call of Duty: Black Ops 6's Steam page to confirm some in-game content has been generated by AI.

In his Bluesky post, Dias offered clarification on several points in an effort to “combat misinformation.”

“While some have characterized the LLM usage as just fixing one individual blocking bug, this is a bit inaccurate; the description given by House House mentioned there being several instances, though they did also say it was mostly "consultative" use and not code gen or agents.”

In the final stretch of Big Walk’s development, LLMs were used sparingly by House House to help work through difficult technical challenges and bugs.

He added that this disclosure prompted a lot of discussion within IGF’s internal forums, some of which Dias found disingenuous or dumb, but he has no intention of outing anyone for their opinions or discussing the matter further. He also noted that, at least as far as his understanding of it goes, Big Walk’s particular usage of AI is not in violation of Steam’s AI disclosure policies, which are slightly different from those used by IGF.

As has frequently been the case in the last couple of years, this particular late-stage disclosure hasn’t gone over great with players. While Big Walk’s overall rating on Steam still remains at Overwhelmingly Positive, at the time of writing, an outpouring of negative reviews have begun to crop up, each expressing disappointment that House House waited as long as it did after the game’s very successful release to disclose its AI usage.

This entire event is rather similar to what transpired with the Indie Game Awards last year, when Clair Obscur: Expedition 33 had its title of Game of the Year stripped after the developers admitted to a similarly small usage of AI in the game’s development.

The specter of generative AI usage isn’t just limited to the deluge of overtly vibe-coded slop that’s been flooding Steam and itch.io recently; even the smallest usage of AI, especially when it’s not disclosed from the jump, can feel extremely disingenuous to players, doubly so in the indie scene, which prides itself on from-scratch, creativity-first game development.

Supermarket Simulator, a new indie game on Steam that recently went viral, has been blasted for its potential use of AI imagery.
