Mamdani gives nonprofits first dibs on buying apartment buildings

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Mayor Zohran Mamdani signed COPA, requiring owners to notify the city and qualified nonprofits before selling covered buildings. The law takes effect in 1 year, as a 38 building portfolio with 850 mostly rent stabilized units heads to foreclosure auctions.  AI Summary

New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani signed legislation Tuesday giving vetted nonprofits a first shot at buying troubled city apartment buildings.

The new measure arrived too late to spare hundreds of rent-stabilized units from foreclosure, a second loss in Mamdani’s bid to protect such units.

Mamdani ran for office and won on creating and protecting affordable housing. National attention has since focused on his efforts to build more housing and cut red tape. His political inspiration and influence have spread to other parts of the country.

Nithya Raman is a prime example. Under a similar affordability theme, the Los Angeles City Council member, also a member of the Democratic Socialists of America, ousted an incumbent to become L.A. mayor.

The Community Opportunity to Purchase Act, known as COPA, requires owners of covered buildings to notify the city and qualified nonprofits before selling. Those groups then get time to make an offer and a right to match a later one. The law takes effect one year after enactment, after the Department of Housing Preservation and Development writes rules.

“This law will give New Yorkers a tool to keep their homes out of the hands of predatory investors seeking to make a quick and easy buck,” Mamdani said at a signing event Tuesday.

He also won in court last week. A Brooklyn appellate panel ruled Nov. 4 that the administration can resume collecting its pied-à-terre tax on high-end second homes, reversing a Staten Island judge who found the rollout flawed. Mamdani says the tax could raise $500 million a year. Other legal challenges remain.

But his administration could not stop the sale of a 38-building portfolio with 850 mostly rent-stabilized apartments. Spatial Equity, an affordable housing developer with City Hall’s backing, failed to close a deal to buy the portfolio before an East Harlem auction began Tuesday afternoon.

The 38 buildings belonged to bankrupt Emerald Equities Group. Banks and other institutions that control about $250 million in mortgage loans will foreclose on each building over three auction days.

Lenders rejected Spatial Equity’s initial $68 million bid, according to a City Limits report. They also turned down a $110 million offer last month, tenant leader Cara Polte told Gothamist.

Polte said tenants will keep pushing for a buyer committed to fixing the buildings. Tenants had hoped to turn her building and four others on the block into a community land trust.

A trust managed by several banks and by Sabal Investment Holdings controls the loans. Freddie Mac partially insured them. Sabal has yet to respond to media requests.

Cea Weaver, director of the Mayor’s Office to Protect Tenants, said the sale does not end the city’s effort to force owners to improve conditions.

This setback follows Mamdani’s first loss on this front. On Jan. 1, he stood in a Pinnacle-owned building and vowed to take on bad landlords. He then sought a 30-day delay of the bankruptcy auction of Pinnacle’s rent-regulated portfolio, hoping to find a buyer other than Summit Properties USA.

A judge allowed the sale to proceed. Summit won about 5,100 mostly rent-stabilized units from Pinnacle, which had filed for Chapter 11 after Flagstar Bank foreclosed.

Emerald bought the 38 buildings for $350 million in 2016 and began raising rents on stabilized apartments. It took out another $180 million loan in December 2018, six months before state lawmakers passed tenant protections that outlawed many of those tactics.

Lenders foreclosed in 2024. Emerald filed for bankruptcy a year later.

A state investigation found Emerald had illegally removed 21 apartments from rent stabilization. The buildings had more than 2,300 housing code violations in April. A retaining wall behind buildings on East 103rd Street collapsed earlier this year, days before Mamdani and Weaver visited in March.

About 1,600 rent-stabilized buildings face financial distress, according to Rent Guidelines Board data. Landlord groups blame rent restrictions, including the board’s two-year rent freeze. Tenant advocates say owners like Emerald borrowed heavily expecting rent increases that later law barred.

In June, Mamdani won a rent freeze through the Rent Guidelines Board for the city’s roughly 1 million stabilized apartments.

Landlords sued to reverse the freeze. The case began in Staten Island, but Justice Ralph Porzio moved it to Manhattan in August, handing Mamdani a partial win. In September, the judge ordered City Hall to turn over communications with the board and signaled he would likely not rule by Oct. 1.

Oct. 1 came and went without a ruling, and the freeze took effect for new and renewing leases.

Meanwhile, a federal lawsuit remains pending over a 2019 state law that limits rents on stabilized units. Small property owners sued in November 2025, arguing the law unconstitutionally keeps stabilized apartments vacant.

COPA’s path was uneven until Mamdani took office. The City Council passed an earlier version in December 2025, but then-Mayor Eric Adams vetoed it, and the Council did not override him. A revised, narrower version passed Oct. 22.

“Progress … is something that we celebrate, and also, we know that it is not inevitable,” Mamdani said. “It has to be won.”

Pablo Neruda on How to Hold Time

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Every month, I spend hundreds of hours and thousands of dollars keeping The Marginalian going. For two decades, it has remained free, ad-free, AI-free, fully human and alive thanks to patronage from readers. I have no staff, no interns, not even an assistant — a thoroughly one-woman labor of love that is also my life and my livelihood. If it makes your own life more livable in any way, please consider aiding its sustenance with a one-time or loyal donation. Your support makes all the difference.

You can also become a spontaneous supporter with a one-time donation in any amount:

Partial to Bitcoin? You can beam some bit-love my way: 197usDS6AsL9wDKxtGM6xaWjmR5ejgqem7

Need to cancel a recurring donation? Go here.

For an act of resistance to the tyranny of algorithms, try the Marginalian newsletter—undistracted notes on the search for meaning, free, ad-free, AI-free, fully human since 2006. (Here is an example.)

“Time is a river that sweeps me along, but I am a river,” Borges wrote. “Time is a fire that consumes me, but I am the fire.”

Most of us are not Borges. Most of us are drowning in bewilderment at where the time goes, burning with the urgency of being alive while waiting to start living, wandering the labyrinth of life with wayward presence, wishing that time ran differently as the cult of productivity turns each minute into a blade pressed against the vein of our transience.

And all the while, our time is nested within our times — the epoch we are living through together, born into it with no more choice in the matter than the body and brain and family we have been born into. In his magnificent essay on Shakespeare, James Baldwin countered the commonplace lament of every epoch: “It is said that his time was easier than ours, but I doubt it — no time can be easy if one is living through it.” A century before him — a century of unrest and transformation — Emerson issued the ultimate antilamentation: “This time, like all times, is a very good one, if we but know what to do with it.”

Not knowing what to do with the time we have been given, not knowing how to hold time in our personal and political lives, is at bottom an act of forgetting how time hold us. Pablo Neruda (July 12, 1904–September 23, 1973) casts a spell against forgetting in the fourth canto of his long poem “Morning,”:

You will remember that whimsical ravine where the vibrant aromas rose, and from time to time a bird dressed in water and languor: winter’s garment.

You will remember those gifts from the earth: piquing fragrance, gold clay, thickets of herbs, wild roots, bewitching thorns like swords.

You will remember the bouquet you brought, a bouquet of shadow and silent water, a bouquet like foam-covered stone.

And that time was like never and always: We go where nothing is expected and find everything waiting there.

If time is the fundamental problem of human life and poetry is our most precise technology for parsing the aching astonishment of being alive, then time is the prime subject of poetry. Neruda knew this — time is the subterranean current coursing beneath his vast and varied body of work, the substrate upon which all of his stunning love poems and his meditations on the inner life grow. He reverenced the stones for how they have “touched time,” reverenced the minute for how it is “bound to join the river of time that bears us,” reverenced “the inexhaustible springs of time,” longed for “a time complete as an ocean,” then made that ocean with his poetry.

In his poem “The Enigmas,” composed during WWII, he writes:

You’ve asked me what the crustacean spins between its gold claws and I reply: the sea knows.

You wonder what the sea squirt waits for in its transparent bell?      What does it wait for?

I’ll tell you: it’s waiting for time like you.

A decade later, in one of his “Elemental Odes,” Neruda laid out his most explicit instruction for how to hold time:

Listen and learn. Time is divided into two rivers: one flows backward, devouring life already lived; the other moves forward with you exposing your life. For a single second they may be joined. Now. This is that moment, the drop of an instant that washes away the past. It is the present. It is in your hands. Racing, slipping, tumbling like a waterfall. But it is yours. Help it grow with love, with firmness, with stone and flight, with resounding rectitude, with purest grains, the most brilliant metal from your heart, walking in the full light of day without fear of truth, goodness, justice, companions of song, time that flows will have the shape and sound of a guitar, and when you want to bow to the past, the singing spring of transparent time will reveal your wholeness. Time is joy.

Couple with three poems for trusting time, then revisit Kahlil Gibran on how to befriend time.

Every month, I spend hundreds of hours and thousands of dollars keeping The Marginalian going. For two decades, it has remained free, ad-free, AI-free, fully human and alive thanks to patronage from readers. I have no staff, no interns, not even an assistant — a thoroughly one-woman labor of love that is also my life and my livelihood. If it makes your own life more livable in any way, please consider aiding its sustenance with a one-time or loyal donation. Your support makes all the difference.

Alex Baena has urged Atletico Madrid to sign Martin Zubimendi and believes the Arsenal midfielder is the second best in the world behind Rodri.

Zubimendi joined Arsenal in a £60 million deal from Real Sociedad last year and the 27-year-old played an integral role in the team’s Premier League title success under Mikel Arteta last season.

However, Zubimendi is yet to start a Premier League game this term and now faces increased competition for a place in Arsenal’s midfield following the arrival of Bruno Guimaraes in the summer transfer window.

Zubimendi has been linked with a move away from Arsenal ahead of the January transfer window and Chelsea are understood to have registered their interest.

Baena, who was also part of Spain’s World Cup success in the summer alongside Zubimendi, is keen to see his countryman return to La Liga.

A Look Back at the Times Kristen Bell and Dax Shepard Defended Their Marriage

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Kristen Bell and Dax Shepard have never been shy about the realities of their relationship.

Since tying the knot in 2013, the couple — who share daughters Lincoln and Delta — have weathered public scrutiny over everything from controversial social media posts to celebrity hot takes about their pairing. Through it all, both Bell and Shepard have consistently shown up for each other and spoken candidly about the work that goes into their marriage.

Keep reading for a look back at the key moments, from most recent to earliest:

Bell and Shepard became the subject of online discourse for the third time in a year when the Nobody Wants This star revealed the surprising way her husband flirts with her: complimenting other people.

“If we go to a dinner or something, like, there’s a couple things Dax does where he doesn’t have to look up and wink at me, but I know that he’s just being the best boy,” she said during an October 2026 episode of Shepard’s podcast. “We’ll be at dinner, and, like, he’ll say to the waiter or waitress, he’ll go, ‘Wow, you have incredible eyelashes, gorgeous eyes, great job.’”

Bell insisted that this is a form of flirting with her because she loves that “he just made someone else feel good.”

“‘Cause he’s gonna have zero interaction with — let’s say it is a girl. He’s gonna have zero interaction with this girl for the rest of his life,” she continued. “The chances of him ever having another conversation with her are so microscopic. But to me, to know that my partner actively smiles at people and gives them compliments [makes me feel good].”

Days after the clip went viral, Bell gushed about his “commitment to growth” during an appearance on The Drew Barrymore Show.

“He’s committed to growth in all aspects,” she said, adding that he’s a “lifelong learner.”

Bell continued, “I call him a vulneraboy. Because he is vulnerable. He knows a lot of stuff, but he’s also not afraid or ashamed to say, ‘I don’t know, I’ve never experienced this before.’ And that’s very sexy.”

Shepard broke his silence on the flirting claims during a November 2026 episode of his podcast, calling out the “weird narrative” spreading online and painting him as “controlling, abusive, now flirting with people in public, which I don’t do.”

“We’re saying I flirt with Kristen by tipping people a lot, and I flirt with Kristen. Yeah, by complimenting people. If you think I’m complimenting hot young waitresses, you got a problem, right?” he explained. “I’m finding the dude that I don’t think has had a compliment in a while and telling him I love his shoes. It’s always somebody that I want to make their day. And so those compliments [are] flirting with Kristen. … I will offer publicly $10 million to any server you can find that I have given my number to, or hit on.”

Shepard said he found the narrative “deeply insulting” to Bell because it implies that she allows emotional abuse around their daughters, Delta and Lincoln. He also noted that he’s “dedicated his whole life” to breaking the cycle of abuse he grew up around.

“I don’t ever yell at my children,” he said. “I don’t swear at them. I don’t yell at my wife. You know, like, to have given it my all to be the opposite of what now some group of people is saying I am is hard, of course. So hard. I don’t love it.”

Shepard concluded, “Generally, I can brush [these controversies] off, but when we’re now on year two of now, there’s a new [one] … then it just starts making me think like, ‘Oh, is this now my f***ing role going forward is just, like, every four months there’s another thing about me being a terrible man. And that makes me want to go away.”

Nearly six months after Bell stirred up controversy with a wedding anniversary tribute, Shepard finally broke his silence on the drama during the Monday, April 6, episode of his “Armchair Expert” podcast with guest Nikki Glaser.

“I was alerted by someone that this whole thing was happening,” Shepard recalled, brushing off the criticism surrounding their marriage after critics of Bell’s October 2025 Instagram post suggested that she walk away and leave their relationship for good. “I didn’t know, and Kristen knew through her publicist but didn’t tell me. So, I actually didn’t know, and this was brought to my attention.”

Shepard faced backlash after Bell gushed over “the man who once said to me: ‘I would never kill you.’ A lot of men have killed their wives at a certain point. Even though I’m heavily incentivized to kill you, I never would.” She shared the controversial message in honor of the special occasion.

Glaser revealed she had initially planned to poke fun at the situation during the 2026 Golden Globe Awards in January 2026, but cut it because she didn’t know where Shepard stood on the topic.

As she prepared to host The Actors Awards, Bell gushed about her husband in an interview with People, noting that he uplifts her spirits and never tries to put her down.

“There’s nothing that he won’t do to take care of me,” Bell said. “One thing that I love so much about being married to him is that he is never allergic to me shining more and more. He never gets insecure about it.”

She added that being married for over a decade is a “long time,” noting, “We put a lot of work into it and I’m very happy. We’re very supportive of each other and our marriage and our family.”

Cher told Shepard and Bell to their faces that she thought Bell could “do better” in terms of finding a romantic partner during a January 2026 appearance on Shepard’s “Armchair Expert” podcast. The Good Place actress didn’t let it go to her head, instead stating that Shepard was “almost too good” for her, telling them both it was a “slow burn” that’s lasted.

During the press tour for season 2 of Nobody Wants This, Bell drew parallels between her marriage and the show’s central romance. “I feel like my real-life husband and I are ‘opposites attract,'” she told People, noting they have grown stronger as a couple despite facing constant scrutiny in the public eye.

That was the same month Bell’s 12th wedding anniversary post to Shepard sparked backlash. The National Network to End Domestic Violence responded to her controversial post, telling Page Six, “There’s nothing funny about domestic violence ‘jokes’ that trivialize the very real fear, trauma and pain that millions of victims and survivors face each day.”

During a conversation with Alicia Silverstone on “Armchair Expert,” Shepard declared he would never speak negatively about his marriage with Bell, even hypothetically.

“I say this all the time, like, if Kristen and I ever split up, I would only describe this as still an enormously successful relationship,” Shepard said. “Like, we loved each other to death. We had these two beautiful children. We had this great time together.”

During an appearance on The View, Bell defended Shepard’s second date move of taking gum from her mouth and chewing it — a story that “divided the hosts” and audience.

“I personally thought it was very cute and it was the indicator, after we had been talking and chatting for awhile and I was like, ‘Oh, this guy’s kinda cute and special,'” Bell said. “And then I guess I was being flirty and took it out of my mouth and then he took it. And I was like, ‘Well…’ I said, ‘Understood.'”

Bell set the record straight after seeing a negative remark about herself and Shepard. “Every piece of news I see about them talks about how they just can’t f***ng stand each other, constantly fight, now this dude is apparently objectifying her as well. just what’s the point?” a social media user wrote via Instagram in February 2021.

Bell fired back at the commenter, “We adore each other, we just try to always be honest about how marriage, or companionship in any form, is hard sometimes.” She added, “You cant always be in control, or right, and its important to us that we lead with the honesty of your ‘ perfect match ‘ being a myth. You gotta work hard to love yourself, and love other humans. Xo.”

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

These complicated business moves helped her network secure thousands of hard-to-get Nvidia GPUs.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang admitted to having an affair with Megaspeed executive Alice Huang, who is also suspected of being a spy working for the Chinese Ministry of State Security (MSS). Now, an investigation by The Information has revealed a 'labyrinth' of companies centered around Shanghai Century Litong and connected to Singaporean firm Megaspeed, which has previously been accused of smuggling $2 billion worth of Nvidia chips. The report claims Alice Huang helped move 240,000 banned chips into the country, mostly H100 and H200 systems, and was instrumental in securing AI GPU imports to the tune of $7.5 billion.

Sources in the report link the two companies to Alice Huang via their personal knowledge and corporate registration records, while there have also been reports that some of the Nvidia AI chips Megaspeed bought went to Shanghai Century Litong’s data centers in China. But aside from these corporate ties, there were also numerous reports about Ms. Huang’s personal relationship with the Nvidia chief, and how it gave her the ability to procure chips that were otherwise hard to get elsewhere.

Ms. Huang allegedly boasted about her access to the Nvidia CEO publicly. People familiar with her said that she regularly uploaded photos with Jensen to her WeChat account and even shared other images with customers to establish her credibility. She even claimed that Jensen Huang would often celebrate her birthday with dinner and gifts, while an Nvidia China sales employee said that she was a familiar face at the company’s annual event for local Chinese partners.

When AI shipments hit delays or faced some trouble, Ms. Huang allegedly would step in to get them moving through the supply chain again. One other person who knows her said that she would often cite her close relationship with Nvidia and its suppliers as the reason why she could always get Nvidia AI chips. It’s estimated that $4.6 billion worth of AI chips have been imported through Megaspeed from 2023 to 2025, with another $2 billion routed through Speedmatrix. It was also reported that Shanghai Century Litong bought GPUs from other sources, with the assistance of Alice Huang if the orders faced an issue, with the total value of the GPUs acquired estimated to be around $7.5 billion. The report claims Ms. Huang helped move some 240,000 banned chips into China.

Alice Huang may have appeared in Jensen Huang’s circle much earlier than previously believed. One source told the publication that Kang Wei, Alice Huang’s husband, met Jensen Huang during the 2010s, when Nvidia was primarily a PC gaming GPU manufacturer. According to the report, Kang Wei was a private equity investor focusing on videogame companies back then. In contrast, Ms. Huang reportedly worked for two media companies in China, although some say that there were no records of her doing so. She eventually jumped into banking and finance, working at HSBC and then CITIC Securities, before finally entering the data center industry in 2023 with Megaspeed’s pivot and the founding of Shanghai Century Litong.

Shanghai Century Litong is a data center operator founded in June 2023 by a shareholder that was ultimately under the control of Huang Yu, the sister of Alice Huang, whose Chinese name is Huang Le. The company then provided compute capacity to AI tech giants in China, including Alibaba, Tencent, and other smaller labs. A few months after its founding, it received coveted Nvidia Preferred Cloud Partner status in October 2023, which is an important milestone as Nvidia requires anyone with this status to have demonstrated sufficient capability in handling and running Nvidia hardware as well as generating revenue at a certain level, although that threshold varies by region and partner type.

In exchange for this, partners get better access to Nvidia’s limited number of chips, which became crucial for Shanghai Century Litong as the China-specific A800 and H800 chips were banned for sale to China around the same period. Although the move immediately banned the sales of these GPUs from Nvidia, it did not affect the chip stocks that were already in the hands of distributors. This meant that Shanghai Century Litong had priority access to the limited supply of these AI GPUs as Nvidia’s preferred partner.

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

Megaspeed, on the other hand, was reportedly owned by a company called Huang Le Limited and led by Ms. Huang when it pivoted in 2023 and was subsequently sold to Singaporean entity Swiftdata soon after this. Megaspeed then set up a subsidiary called Speedmatrix, which has two offices in Johor, Malaysia, a couple of months after that. The Bureau of Industry and Security (BIS), the U.S. agency in charge of monitoring export control compliance, noted that the Nvidia hardware found in Speedmatrix data centers was still in its boxes when they inspected the sites, triggering a deeper investigation into the company. Still, it’s been alleged that Nvidia intervened in the BIS probe, with the company retaining its Nvidia partner status to this day.

Despite her public closeness with the Nvidia chief, she’s rather elusive when it comes to her personal information. Two other people said that she has always been vague about what she does at her companies, and that she’s careful when it comes to signing documents and leaving a paper trail that could easily be followed. Still, the Chinese embassy denies any links to Alice Huang, even as the FBI looks into the situation.

Ohio 'House of Horrors' Mom Is Pregnant, Ruled Competent to Stand Trial

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The attorney for Ohio “House of Horrors” mom Elizabeth Siders confirmed to Us that his client is pregnant after being arrested for allegedly endangering her 16 kids and forcing them to live in unkempt conditions.

Siders’ pregnancy was revealed during a hearing on Monday, November 9, where a Vinton County judge ruled that she is competent to stand trial.

Her attorney, Thomas Stolly, has not further commented on how far along she is or when her due date is.

An hour before the judge ruled that Elizabeth, 34, was competent to stand trial, her mother-in-law, Christina Siders, was deemed not competent to stand trial.

“Elizabeth was found competent to stand trial. Both sides stipulated to the report; Judge accepted. I noted my issues with the evaluation. Specifically, that the evaluator only met with Elizabeth for 1 hour 30 minutes via zoom,” Stolly said in a statement to Us. “The Court order re: competency requires an in person meeting. (2) I am requesting a second evaluation/funding for a neuropsychological expert to evaluate Elizabeth for her NGRI defense; that is, her competency at the time the alleged offenses occurred.”

Stolly continued, “I noted issues that I found in that report as well. Most specifically, that the evaluator seemingly conducted both evaluations in the same 1 hour 30 minute zoom interview and noting inconsistencies in the conclusions vs. the comments in the evaluation.”

“More specifically, that the evaluator has recognized Elizabeth’s current mental health diagnoses, but did not comment about how they could have potentially impacted her mental health/condition at the time of the alleged offenses,” Stolly said. “A 1 hour 30 minute zoom evaluation is wholly insufficient to determine the history/severity of Elizabeth’s mental condition and the potential impact on her mental state at the time of the alleged offenses.”

Christina, 68, Elizabeth and her husband, Gary Siders Jr., and his father, Gary Siders Sr., have all been charged with child endangerment after they were arrested on June 30 when authorities learned the conditions of their home.

All four family members have pleaded not guilty to the charges. Gary Sr., 73, was previously declared incompetent to stand trial in September.

In addition to the child endangerment charges, Elizabeth and Gary Jr., 36, have been indicted on additional charges after they were accused of “engaging in sexual conduct with a minor more than 10 years younger than their own age.” They have also pleaded not guilty to those charges.

A $250,000 cash surety bond and a $300,000 bond have been set for Elizabeth in light of her charges. She has also been ordered to have no contact with her husband or the victims in the case.

If you or someone you know is experiencing child abuse, call or text Child Help Hotline at 1-800-422-4453.

AI-assisted firms increase flaw disclosures by 120% with no detected repair slowdown, study finds

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

A preprint found a roughly 120% relative increase in disclosures against a Chinese-firm comparison group, with no detectable slowdown in the repair channels studied.

Software-flaw disclosures at firms given early access to Anthropic's AI model Claude Mythos Preview rose by roughly 120% relative to the change at Chinese firms without access, a November 4 preprint finds. Measured repair times showed no detectable slowdown.

Thomas Mosk's study finds that AI-assisted bug hunting was associated with more public disclosures without a detectable slowdown in the repair channels studied. Its findings cover publicly disclosed vulnerabilities and specific repair channels, leaving undisclosed workloads and users' installation of patches unmeasured.

Anthropic announced Project Glasswing on April 7, giving selected firms access to the model. The main disclosure analysis compares 13 participants with 267 Chinese firms from October 2025 through September 2026. The Chinese firms could use other AI tools. Selection into Glasswing was not random, and national disclosure practices differ, limiting causal interpretation.

Alongside the 120% relative increase in disclosures, the paper estimates roughly 150% more high-impact disclosures. That category means an above-median impact component in the vulnerability severity score.

Earlier Epoch AI analysis, published July 2, documented a spike in high- and critical-severity disclosures after Mythos Preview. It also warned that public counts omit undisclosed discoveries and can reflect increased interest in bug hunting.

Mosk examines fixing capacity separately, comparing Glasswing vulnerabilities with other non-Chinese vulnerabilities in shorter samples. One analysis follows 658 flaws reported to vendors by the Zero Day Initiative through June 2026. Another covers 14,855 vulnerabilities published through July and tracked by Linux distributions including Debian, Ubuntu, Red Hat and SUSE.

Neither repair channel showed a statistically detectable slowdown. For disclosed flaws, the distributions' median time to a fix stayed at one day. The vendor estimates rule out a slowdown larger than about 13%, although smaller changes remain possible. The downstream estimates still permit an approximately 11 percentage-point decline in the share fixed within 90 days.

For crypto infrastructure, the practical distinction is between finding a flaw, releasing a fix and installing it. CryptoSlate reported on Oct. 29 that Bitcoin Core disclosed three AI-discovered flaws after validation and patch releases. Installation across nodes remains a separate question.

The exploitation results also require care. In a comparison with large non-Chinese firms ending in May, before Glasswing's June expansion, Mosk found no statistically significant change in exploited-vulnerability counts across one-, two- and three-month windows.

The share of disclosed flaws known to be exploited fell in some comparisons. A larger pool of disclosures can lower that share without reducing exploited-vulnerability counts, however. Mosk treats the probability decline as suggestive; the count estimates remain too imprecise to exclude moderate changes in either direction.

Reported disclosure and exploitation estimates use different samples and comparison groups.

Why Everyone Is Suddenly Talking About Van Deer Skis

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

Backed by Red Bull and fresh off Paula Moltzan’s first World Cup win, Van Deer is turning up in shops across North America. Here’s what we know about the skis, the price and the market. (Photo: Getty Images)

Overview: Van Deer is a ski brand created by racing legend Marcel Hirscher and majority-owned by Red Bull. Launched in 2021, it makes World Cup race, groomer, freeride and touring skis, priced from about $1,000 to $1,700. Paula Moltzan won her first World Cup race on Van Deer skis in Sölden. Distribution in North America is still limited, but the brand plans to open more storefronts this year.

If you watched the opening women’s World Cup GS in Sölden, Austria, you probably did a double take at Paula Moltzan’s skis. The U.S. racer won by 0.01 for her first career World Cup victory, and she did it on Van Deer-Red Bull Sports, a nascent brand she switched to this season. Van Deer is the brainchild of ski-racing GOAT Marcel Hirscher, and Moltzan’s win brought instant attention to a brand with a unique origin story.

The brand launched in 2021 and gained notoriety in 2022 with Norwegian Henrik Kristoffersen’s success on its skis, including a slalom crystal globe, an Olympic medal and a World Championship gold. Now Van Deer skis are suddenly turning up everywhere in places like Deer Valley and Vail—and even at big retailers like Evo. They’re not cheap: prices run from about $1,000 to $1,700, but it seems skiers are willing to pay. “Last year we had a phenomenal year with Van Deer skis,” said Billy Schoff, equipment buyer at Gorsuch, a high-end ski shop, which has sold them for three seasons. “We get people out on them once; they love them and then the word spreads like wildfire.”

So what is Van Deer, who are its skis for, and where does it fit in the North American market? Here’s what to know.

The details of Van Deer are deeply woven into what’s meaningful to Hirscher, who originally retired in 2019 (he came out of retirement in 2024, only to tear his ACL and be sidelined again). The brand’s name is a witty wordplay on his heritage—he’s half Dutch, half Austrian, and the German word “hirsch” means deer, hence: Van Deer. The H-Power 68 ski model nods to Hirscher’s Project 68, his initiative to get another win after his 67 career World Cup wins, even if he’s not the one racing anymore.

As a startup ski brand, it’s incredibly difficult to compete in a saturated ski market full of legacy brands, but along with Hirscher’s passion and precision, Van Deer has something other brands struggle with—resources. Hirscher was a Red Bull-sponsored athlete and maintains a strong relationship with the company. The partnership with Red Bull Sports, a multi-billion-dollar global brand, affords Van Deer-Red Bull Sports the ability to build their own factory stocked with state-of-the-art machines, hire top people to work for them, and support top athletes like Moltzan. (Red Bull has a 51 percent majority stake in Van Deer.)

“Marcel is obsessed with building the best-quality and best-selling skis,” said Schoff, who along with Dave Gorsuch was brought in early on Hirscher’s project. Moltzan sees the Red Bull effect from the athlete side: “Van Deer is still a new brand, so having Red Bull behind it makes a big difference. They provide a lot of support, which lets us focus on building the best skis we can.”

From the outside, Moltzan’s move looked like a risk. She had skied for Rossignol for 18 years and was coming off a season with four second-place World Cup finishes and an Olympic medal. “I know the change might look risky from the outside, but I was ready for it,” she said. “I was looking for new motivation and inspiration, and the chance to work with a team that has the ability and resources to build equipment specifically for me was a huge draw.”

She didn’t have a direct conversation with Hirscher before making the switch—they chatted briefly when she arrived at the factory. “I was going to be the only woman on the skis, and I knew there was a lot of work ahead of us, but that was also part of what made it exciting,” she said. “The first time I got on them, they felt completely different from anything I’d skied before, but I could immediately tell they were going to be good.”

Her husband and longtime serviceman, Ryan Mooney, was sold at the factory. “Ryan was really impressed when we toured the factory, the level of precision during the build process is certainly something that sets the brand apart from others,” Moltzan said. “He thought the powerful nature of the ski could be a great match for my style of skiing.”

True to its racing roots, every Van Deer ski has a World Cup construction and finish, but the brand now goes well beyond the race course with wider waist widths. Today there are five series:

Van Deer’s consumer skis are all unisex for now. On the race side, Moltzan says Van Deer didn’t start from an existing ski. “We’ve built completely new skis for the women’s side, so my feedback has been part of the process from the beginning,” she said, along with input from Mooney and Berni Arnitz, her second technician, who oversees women’s development. What makes them different? “Women’s skis are different from men’s skis, including the length, shape, and construction,” she said. “But I don’t think it needs to be more complicated than that.”

For now, finding the right ski comes down to length and model. “There are shorter sizes like 147 in H-Power 68 and 159 in H-Power 78,” says Max Wingard, Van Deer-Red Bull Sports race and promotions manager. “The SL model is the ski we’d sell as an easier to ski slalom ski. The GS Pro could be a masters ski, but comes in longer lengths.”

We haven’t had a chance to test the skis ourselves yet, but according to those who’ve tried them, they’re not just for racers. “I think the best customer for Van Deer is someone who really loves skiing and cares about their equipment,” Moltzan said. “You don’t have to be a racer. There are skis for everything from race courses to touring, but they’re built for people who want high performance and appreciate the details that go into a great pair of skis.”

Eric Lipton, National Alpine Team alumnus and director of Destination Ski Camps, skied the HP-78 and described it as “predictable and stable at speed.” “I’d describe the skis as having a race ski heart and soul in a consumer package,” he said.

The North American hardgoods market is a different animal from Europe’s piste-focused market, and Van Deer is making a clear play for more traction in the U.S. According to Schoff, the brand plans to build a North American warehouse, and it has recently hired George Couperthwait, formerly of Stöckli, to help build the brand and its dealer network here.

Schoff sees the target clearly. “I spent time in Austria with Leon (Hirscher’s brother, formerly of Atomic who now works on product for Van Deer) and his father, Ferdinand,” he said. “They were obsessed with building unbelievable race products and now the obsession includes commercial skis. They are doing it the old craftsman way, but with new machines that put on the best finish and even rip the sidewalls back. The quality of construction and finish is top quality and it’s clear they are going after high-end brands like Stöckli.”

Distribution is still limited, says Wingard, but the goal this year is to open more storefronts and grow the relationship with their existing retailers as well.

Wingard, who previously worked for Marker Völkl Dalbello and Stöckli, thinks Van Deer will catch on in both the East and the West. The line includes multiple narrow-waisted skis tailor-made for East Coast conditions. Out West, Van Deer will be pushing the Freeride 98 and 108, with former World Cup racer brand ambassador Travis Ganong giving feedback on the series and stores like Gorsuch in Vail, Aspen and Park City on board.

As a ski coach and former racer, I have a fondness for racey skis in a consumer package. You can feel the high-end construction and finish right out of the box, even in the way the bases glide on snow. A close comparison is Stöckli. For years, Stöckli skis have been a favorite among ski instructors, because skiers who focus on carving, like me, tend to gravitate toward skis with rebound energy, edge grip and stability. Van Deer shares Stöckli’s high cost and a degree of exclusivity, but the top-grade race materials and hand-tuning steps are meant to justify the price.

Van Deer’s advantage is that it has pulled together European craftsmanship, enticing long-time legacy-brand employees to come aboard, with state-of-the-art ski technology like the Wintersteiger Jupiter tuning machine and a brand-new factory, thanks to Red Bull’s resources.

All models except the Freeride 108 (which has some carbon) and the Touring series have a game-on construction: a double layer of Titanal and a woodcore of poplar, ash or beech. The Van Deer models available this season best suit strong intermediate to expert skiers who can drive a ski through a turn transition and want the ski to initiate a turn without too much effort, while still feeling edge grip underfoot and acceleration out of the turn.

You don’t have to ski like Paula Moltzan to appreciate a pair of Van Deers, but perhaps you aspire to turn like she does.

Current models are available online as well as at speciality retailers. Skiers interested in demoing or buying a pair can find a dealer on the company website. As for Moltzan, she’s racing around the globe, with Levi, Finland, next, followed by Killington, Vermont. As good as the skis already are, she says, they’re still a work in progress: “Skis are always evolving, so there will definitely be more changes to come.”

Half of New Games on Steam Are Now Made With AI. How Does This Reflect the Gaming Industry?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Most of those games are unpopular, with 10 or fewer reviews from gamers.

Steam, the digital marketplace for PC games from game studio Valve, has hit a major milestone, sparking pushback from parts of the gaming community: Approximately 50% of new Steam games in October were made with the help of AI.

This underscores a growing trend in gaming, where more games are made with some form of AI assistance — specifically, generative AI, which developers have used to generate code, art assets, voice lines and more. Developers are adopting the technology in increasing numbers.

The numbers come from a Reddit user who goes by Ploz, and who used Steam’s publicly available data to see how many newly released games relied on AI in some capacity. This was possible because of Valve’s AI disclosure policy for newly released games, which requires developers to state whether they used AI in making their game. The disclosure is part of the storefront’s publicly accessible records, allowing anyone to view it.

A Valve representative did not respond to a request for comment.

The data is a snapshot of the tidal wave of AI-made games flooding storefronts, most prominently Steam. But crucially, Valve has chosen not to limit this flood, washing its hands of the problem and leaving gamers to fend for themselves in finding quality games. Developers had already been struggling to get their games noticed, and Valve’s limited curation seems completely unprepared for the firehose of AI-made games churned out on an increasing scale.

Valve implemented the AI disclosure policy in January 2024 to increase developer transparency. At the time, only 1.4% of newly released games used AI. This increased to 13.4% by the end of 2024, then to 24% by the end of 2025. It now sits at 49.9%.

There are some caveats. The AI disclosure is only required for games that use AI to create player-facing content, such as artwork, sound, character dialogue and localization. The 49.9% figure is specific to these types of games and doesn’t account for developers who use AI for behind-the-scenes tasks such as coding and bug fixes. A Google Cloud survey conducted in August 2025 showed that 87% of developers use AI for backend work, which doesn’t require disclosure on Steam.

So it’s likely that the number of games on Steam built with AI assistance is greater than 50%. Ploz notes that the only way users can avoid AI-generated games is to manually check for AI disclosures.

According to Ploz, the most common use of AI in game development is for artwork, which was mentioned in 63% of disclosures. Text and translations gobbled up 30%, while music (21%), code (16%) and voices (7%) rounded out the list. Many developers had more than one of those areas named in their disclosure, and about a quarter of developers said they were using AI for their Steam store pages and marketing.

About 3% of developers used AI directly as a game mechanic. One such example is a driving simulator called Teach My Little Sister How to Drive. Developers use AI in the game to interpret a player’s voice commands and generate the sister’s responses. To keep a playable demo of the game available to the public, the development team, Easy Fox, is paying $1,000 every day just to cover these AI operating costs — an expense so high that it was forced to take out a loan simply to keep the demo running.

For development, there are loads of options. The Unity 6 engine came out in October 2024 and includes AI code assistance, AI asset generation and third-party integration with Claude Code, Gemini and Grok. Anthropic’s Claude is a fairly popular option among smaller developers, since it supports the ability to vibe code whole games, with ChatGPT, Gemini and GitHub Copilot as other options.

Many of the games being released with the AI disclosure on Steam can be classified as slop — a derogatory term gamers have applied liberally to titles that heavily lean on generative AI for development and look derivative of other popular games. According to Ploz, most AI-enabled games are small: Around 47% of new games with fewer than 10 reviews have an AI disclosure. Among developers who released more than 10 games in a year, 68% of those games included the disclosure.

Games with AI disclosures received fewer positive reviews on average than those without. And among all newly released games with more than 1,000 reviews, only 17% include an AI disclosure. That means gamers are still gravitating toward games without an AI disclosure and pushing back against games that use the technology.

Developers seem aware of this. CNET reached out to two dozen well-known developers, including Epic Games, EA, Activision Blizzard, Ubisoft, PlayStation Studios and many others. All of them declined to comment.

Bigger studios may be using AI in their development, but it’s hard to tell the scale. There’s no such thing as an AI audit yet, and the industry seems to be relying on voluntary disclosure for now. Steam’s disclosure policy has outed smaller developers who are either worried about violating the policy and being taken off the digital storefront, or aren’t worried about the negative effects of disclosure.

A lot of dominoes had to fall for this to happen. The availability of AI tools and the increasing ease of use are definite contributors. Subreddits like r/AIGameDev have plenty of examples of people putting together games with just a few days or weeks of vibe coding, which is a lot shorter than the standard video game development cycle, which can stretch over years depending on the scale, production level and graphics quality. This practice has exploded over the last couple of years, resulting in a lot of games getting launched that are made mostly or entirely with AI.

Many of these games end up on Steam, which doesn’t have a policy limiting AI-developed games.

“Digital storefronts like Steam have already struggled to moderate low-effort and poor-quality games, as well as copycats stealing other games’ ideas,” said CNET Managing Editor David Lumb.

Valve openly admits its role in this, stating that the problem definitely exists but that the company doesn’t “want to be gatekeepers telling developers which tools they should or shouldn’t use.”

It takes a similar stance on AI cloning, with Valve’s Tom Giardino telling developers that “you must continue to grow your game by adding features, personality and creativity that stem from your own perspective as a developer.”

These policies essentially allow any AI-developed game or clone to reach the Valve storefront without pushback, which certainly contributes to the flood of newly released games with some form of player-facing AI involvement in their development.

Rival store Epic Games has a similar stance, allowing basically any game that wants to be there. The company declined to comment further about this, but CEO Tom Sweeney is an avid supporter of AI in video game development, stating that it was “perfectly logical” for developers to rely on AI to handle the mundane grunt work that takes up most of a developer’s time.

While Steam and Epic Games tend to take a more lax attitude toward AI slop, other storefronts like Itch.io are not. Itch.io has a whole policy against AI slop and the company has willingly, and at times gleefully, deleted terabytes of AI slop from its storefront. It also requires developers to disclose if they used AI but takes a much more consumer-friendly approach by letting users filter those out of search results if they don’t want to see them.

Some measures are being taken to address the proliferation of AI slop, but none will entirely stop the problem. Cloning has been a problem for many years. Mobile gamers of a certain vintage may remember the flock of Flappy Bird clones that appeared on app stores after the original game went down, and those weren’t even made with AI.

AI makes cloning much easier and faster, and some AI companies are addressing it. Anthropic recently changed Claude Opus 5.5 to prevent it from generating copies of existing games. Anthropic engineering leader Addy Osmani posted on X that using Claude to copy games infringes on intellectual property rights and violates Anthropic’s policy.

However, during CNET’s testing, we found we could still make games resembling popular titles, so cloning remains possible with a little creativity.

“This has come up over and over on the subreddit r/GameDev, and there’s no real solution if Steam and other digital storefronts don’t stem the flood of low-quality AI games drowning out the labors of love that entertain and innovate,” Lumb said.

Many developers openly oppose AI and refuse to use it in their own work. However, as the Steam stats show, that crowd’s output appears to be getting smaller and smaller every year amid the unending stream of AI games.

Ripple CEO: Big XRP Holdings Don’t Mean Control

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Home » Ripple (XRP) » Ripple CEO: Big XRP Holdings Don’t Mean Control

Security measures within XRP’s native ledger ensure no holder on the rich-list gets to take over the chain.

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse used the company’s Swell 2026 event to address a long-running claim that Ripple Labs effectively controls the XRP Ledger simply because it holds a large amount of XRP. He said ownership of the token does not confer authority over network rules or validation.

The XRP Ledger currently has more than 150 validators. Of the 35 on the current recommended unique node list, only one is operated by Ripple, roughly 2.9 percent.

Network operators decide which validators they trust and can configure their own lists rather than relying solely on any single published set.

Unlike proof-of-stake systems, XRP holdings do not grant extra voting weight. Independent validators reach agreement on transactions. Protocol amendments require at least 80 percent validator support for two consecutive weeks before they activate.

BOOOM! $XRP Holders🚨🚨🚨 @bgarlinghouse CEO of Ripple debunked one of the biggest misconceptions about XRP ledger!For everyone still claiming Ripple controls the XRP Ledger because it owns billions of XRP, listen to what Brad said at Ripple Swell 2026.Owning XRP does not… https://t.co/zyDgmkPmIM pic.twitter.com/mhpDrqLnXG

Ripple can propose software changes and invest in the ecosystem, but it cannot unilaterally force upgrades.

Three amendments have been activated this year: Permissioned Domains on February 4, Permissioned DEX on February 18, and Batch transactions on October 9, 2026.

Notably, these tools are designed to support more controlled environments for institutional applications while still operating on the shared ledger.

Several highly established financial firms have moved forward with XRP-related products. Aviva Investors launched a tokenized share class of its Sterling Liquidity Fund. Franklin Templeton, DBS Bank, and Ripple have outlined plans involving tokenized fund shares and RLUSD.

Standard Chartered has also announced live institutional custody of RLUSD on the ledger, with additional collateral work planned alongside Ripple and LMAX Group.

These bullish developments do not depend on Ripple controlling the ledger. They rely on the existing consensus rules, low transaction costs, and the ability of independent parties to issue assets and settle on the network.

Even without control of validation, XRP retains specific functions on the ledger. Transaction fees, currently as low as 0.00001 XRP, are destroyed. Funded accounts require a base reserve.

The OG token can also serve as an intermediary asset for bridging different issued currencies when that path offers better execution.

Mr. Garlinghouse’s comments restate a technical distinction that has been part of XRPL design for years: large XRP holdings do not equal governance power.

Lotus’s New V-8 Ferrari Fighter Is Coming in 2028; Here’s the Engine That Will Power It

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Horse Powertrains has pulled the wraps off its first eight-cylinder powerplant, destined for the Lotus Type 135.

Back in May, Lotus revealed its Focus 2030 strategy for the future, in which the storied British racing and sports car brand aims to return to its roots by “protecting what makes Lotus, Lotus.” What makes Lotus Lotus is, of course, at least partially combustion-powered performance cars that follow Colin Chapman’s maxim to “simplify and add lightness.” As a thrilling nod in that direction, Lotus confirmed plans to launch its first supercar, the Type 135—which may become the new Esprit.

Now, we now know what will power it. On Tuesday, Horse Powertrain—a Geely subsidiary alongside Lotus—unveiled its new Horse W40 powerplant, which the company has confirmed the will power the Lotus Type 135. Horse also manufactures the new V-6 engine that will replace the supercharged Toyota V-6 in the Emira sports car.

The Horse W40 is a twin-turbocharged 4.0-liter V8 with a hot-vee configuration, designed for use in high-performance hybrid vehicle platforms. It weighs just 441 pounds, but it can produce an impressive peak output of over 670 horsepower and 590 pound-feet of torque all on its own. When paired with a hybrid component, Lotus said the Type 135 will be able to deliver more than 986 hp.

The Horse W40 uses a proprietary 8LDCT transmission. It’s an eight-speed transmission with a “sportscar-grade wet dual clutch.” Horse Powertrain claims a shift response time of less than 20 milliseconds, and the transmission can handle up to 774 pound-feet of torque.

Lotus has not yet provided full details about the Type 135—those will be announced before the end of 2026—but the company has confirmed it plans to launch the new machine in 2028. The Type 135 will be all-new, distinct from the Emira; Lotus expects to build the supercar in Europe, unlike the China-made Eletre and Emeya EVs, which may reduce the potential exposure to American tariffs. Lotus has revealed a teaser image of the new car, showing a dual exhaust and a large diffuser.

The Lotus Type 135 may not be the only application for this engine, either. Horse Powertrain says the engine’s low-end torque could be used for trucks and off-road vehicles, if you’ve been keeping a candle lit for Geely-owned Volvo to build a raucous but Scandinavian minimalist Mercedes G-Wagen competitor. exposed steel trusses and corrugated metal envelope shape riverfront kogaa’s šmeral art factory

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

by opening the formerly inaccessible complex to the riverside, the project extends its role beyond the gallery itself, contributing to the ongoing transformation of the svitava river corridor and to the cultural life of the city.

LINKA News edited by christina vergopoulou November 10, 2026 4 min

Designed by architecture studio KOGAA as part of its ongoing Making Use research initiative, ŠMERAL ART FACTORY is an adaptive reuse project converting a decommissioned industrial production hall into a cultural venue in Brno, Czech Republic. Sited on the banks of the Svitava River within the historic Šmeral industrial complex, the project introduces exhibitions and public programming while maintaining the structural and material identity of the former manufacturing site.

The spatial sequence begins externally along the riverside cycle and pedestrian path, guiding visitors past the Šmeral Graffiti Wall, Brno’s first designated legal graffiti wall, before entering the factory yard. The building’s original steel framework and overhead ventilation ducts have been retained and adapted into the active air distribution network, keeping the technical services legible alongside refurbished original light fixtures updated with LED components. Overhead, KOGAA’s design team reclad the roof in corrugated metal sheeting echoing the original industrial envelope, integrating enlarged roof lights that reduce structural dead loads while funneling natural daylight into the hall.

Internally, the volume is cleared to form continuous white perimeter exhibition walls. Circulation and building services are organized around a central freestanding volume: externally, it reads as a white monolithic block housing the reception, bar, and additional display surfaces; internally, it conceals visitor amenities within an all-black core. Bespoke millwork, including a corrugated-profile reception desk, was fabricated directly on-site within the Šmeral workshops. Outside, an operational historic stamping press stands in dialogue with open-air artworks, complemented by a heritage factory-green finish applied to roof trims, chimneys, and window reveals.

lead architects: Tomáš Kozelský, Viktor Odstrčilík, Alexandra Georgescu

design team: Zoja Doupovcová, Markéta Landová, Kinga Sredzinska

client: ŠMERAL ART FACTORY | @smeralartfactory

refurbishment of original light fixtures: Alexis Raverdy

location: Křenová 261/65c, Brno, Czech Republic

photographer: Alex Shoots Buildings | @alex.shoots.buildings

Arctic Town Kills Dozens of Grizzlies Feeding on Dump

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

For years, the grizzlies near Inuvik in Canada’s western Arctic relied on a stable source of food — the town dump. Generations of bears grew up feeding there. In 2025, wildlife officials found that over 40 grizzlies regularly visited the dump.

In 2025, before the fence was built, trail cameras captured multiple grizzlies hanging around the dump, looking for food. Photo: Government of the Northwest Territories

It was too much. This year, the town built an electric fence around the site, hoping the bears would move on. They didn’t. Some started coming into town looking for food. Others just hung around the fence, searching for a way in.

“They would just do these laps around the dump, and they would just be emaciated, sickly,” said Toby Halle, a Renewable Resources officer based in Inuvik, told the CBC. “They don’t know what to do with themselves. And quite frankly, we don’t know what to do with them.”

Inuvik, population about 3,500, was the first community in that part of the Arctic to try to wean the bears off their dependence on dump food with such a fence, which cost about $700,000. The problem was, the bears had not learned to depend on any other source of food.

“We’ve probably had three to four generations of bears that have been using that landfill,” said local mayor Peter Clarkson. In the end, the town took a drastic solution — but one that was probably unavoidable in the circumstances: They shot 28 bears.

Tragic though this is, officials hope that within the next couple of years, the remaining bears in the area will move on and learn to eat more natural fare.

Many other Arctic towns still have open dumps where bears come to feed. Photo: Government of the Northwest Territories

Jerry Kobalenko is the editor of ExplorersWeb. One of Canada’s premier arctic travelers, he is the author of The Horizontal Everest and Arctic Eden, and has just finished a book about adventures in Labrador. In 2018, he was awarded the Polar Medal by the Governor General of Canada and in 2022, he received the Queen’s Platinum Jubilee Medal for services to exploration.

ExplorersWeb is part of AllGear Digital's portfolio of media brands.

‘Big prospect’ – Teddy Sheringham urges Man Utd to sign £30m Brighton star ahead of Arsenal

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Teddy Sheringham believes Manchester United should look to sign Brighton striker Charalampos Kostoulas to add more firepower to their side.

The Red Devils currently sit 18th in the Premier League table and without a win in eight matches in the competition after their dramatic 4-4 draw with Aston Villa last weekend.

And that disappointing start to the season appears set to cost Michael Carrick his job, with the latest reports suggesting the 45-year-old will be sacked over the international break.

Eddie Howe, Fabian Hurzeler, Julian Nagelsmann and Antonio Conte have all been linked with Manchester United over the past couple of months.

But former Tottenham and Chelsea boss Mauricio Pochettino has now emerged as a serious candidate to replace Carrick before their match against Liverpool on November 22.

Read exclusive analysis of the biggest stories in football with Metro's weekly football newsletter In The Mixer. Sign up now for free.

Regardless of who ultimately replaces Carrick, Sheringham believes the new United manager will need support in the transfer market to turn his former club’s fortunes around.

In particular, the former England striker believes the Red Devils could do with some more attacking firepower, name-checking young Brighton striker Kostoulas as his ideal target in the January window.

‘There’s one player I’d like to see Manchester United go and sign in January and it’s the young Greek centre-forward at Brighton, Charalampos Kostoulas,’ Sheringham told Metro. ‘What a player he is. I love him.

‘I love the way he plays. He’s got energy, he knows his position, and he’s still a young man as well.

Kostoulas, who arrived from Olympiakos for £30million in June 2025, has five goal contributions in ten league matches this season to help his side sit second in the table.

‘I know he’s only a young lad but he’s just got that stature about him, and he’s full of confidence. Confidence is a massive thing. He’s playing with confidence right now and at the moment he looks fantastic.’

Équipe de France : La grande surprise de Zidane avec Eli Junior Kroupi !

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Nouveau rebondissement au rassemblement des Bleus ce mardi 10 novembre 2026. Pour pallier le forfait sur blessure de Marcus Thuram, le sélectionneur Zinédine Zidane a décidé d'appeler en renfort Eli Junior Kroupi, qui fête à cette occasion sa toute première convocation chez les A.

À 20 ans, l'attaquant de Bournemouth formé au FC Lorient s'offre une promotion inattendue sous le maillot tricolore. Une trajectoire fulgurante. Présent dans la pré-liste mais absent de la liste principale communiquée le 6 novembre dernier en raison d'un manque de rythme consécutif à une fracture du métatarse, Eli Junior Kroupi n'avait pas non plus été retenu chez les Espoirs par Gérald Baticle.Le jeune avant-centre restait sur une entrée remarquée en Premier League, ponctuée d'une passe décisive en seulement 22 minutes de jeu lors de la victoire contre Leeds United (2-0). Ainsi, la FFF a officialisé ce mardi sa convocation de substitution.

Cette arrivée découle directement du coup d'arrêt subi par Marcus Thuram. L'attaquant de l'Inter Milan, appelé par Zinédine Zidane pour la première fois pour ce rassemblement de novembre après avoir manqué celui de septembre, a été touché à l'ischio droit le dimanche 8 novembre 2026 lors du succès face à Côme (2-0). Le joueur n'a pas pu participer à la séance d'entraînement du lundi soir avec les Bleus, restant aux soins sans constater d'amélioration. Face à cette blessure musculaire, le staff médical tricolore a finalement décidé de le remettre à la disposition de son club dès ce mardi.

Pour Eli Junior Kroupi, cette intégration de dernière minute intervient au cœur d'une Ligue des nations jusqu'ici parfaitement maîtrisée par la sélection tricolore. En effet, l'équipe de France occupe la tête de son groupe après quatre journées, avec un bilan de trois victoires et un match nul. Les Bleus affronteront l'Italie le jeudi 12 novembre 2026 à Milan lors de la 5e journée de la compétition, avant de recevoir la Turquie le dimanche 15 novembre 2026 à Bordeaux pour la 6e journée.

■ An Update to the PlayStation Plus App on PC

We wanted to let you know that from December 10, we will be retiring the PlayStation Plus app on PC.

PlayStation believes in providing high-quality experiences to our players, and we regularly review offerings to ensure we are focusing on experiences our members value most. With a very small number of eligible PlayStation Plus Premium members (less than 0.4 percent) using the PlayStation Plus app on PC, making this change allows us to focus on enhancing high-quality experiences that more of our members want to use. As a result, the PlayStation Plus cloud streaming app on PC will no longer be available.

This change does not affect PlayStation Plus memberships or benefits available on PlayStation 5 and PlayStation 4 consoles. We appreciate everyone who used the app on PC and will be notifying affected users directly.

‘We Have to Take Over’: Far-Right Extremists Want to Seize Control of the GOP

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

On Election Day last week, white nationalist Nick Fuentes urged his supporters on X to “get out and vote Democrat today.” The post racked up over 12 million views. Hours later, in the wake of the Democratic sweep, Fuentes took credit for the victory. “We did it again” he wrote on Telegram, later calling the phenomenon “the Groyper Effect,” a reference to the nickname given to his followers.

In recent days, Fuentes has claimed the election results are an opportunity for him and his allies on the far right.

“Let the Democrats rule; see how that goes,” Fuentes said during a livestream last week. “There will come a time when the far left will terrorize people, and they're going to push all of them into the arms of the right. Who's going to fight the far left? Only the far right. This is why we have to take over the right wing now.”

In another livestream last week, Fuentes added a warning to the GOP leadership: “It’s our party now … You need to cooperate with us… Groypers are open for business. It has [to] be America First—and if it's not, we'll do it again for the enemy.”

A WIRED review of comments from white-supremacist, Christian-nationalist, and other far-right groups in the wake of the midterm results shows much of the same. While the groups are uniformly attacking Donald Trump’s failure to prioritize white, Christian, American values, they also believe that this sudden shift to the left will result in a backlash that will see more people embrace far-right groups and ideologies.

“Many prominent figures in racist, extremist movements have taken to declaring the MAGA movement dead since the midterm elections, claiming that the results validate their previous criticisms of the Trump administration and opens new opportunities for their movements to push disaffected voters toward their ideological beliefs,” Jared Holt, senior researcher at Open Measures, tells WIRED.

While some of the Republican party’s extremist candidates were rejected during the elections last week, many did win their elections.

The Institute for Research and Education on Human Rights tracked 535 candidates running in statewide races who were members of at least one far-right Facebook group. The researchers found that of the 476 races that had been called by last Friday, 338 of those candidates were victorious, representing a 71 percent win rate. (The vast majority of these races saw GOP incumbents retain their seats.) In Florida, for example, 34-year-old citrus farmer Marshall Rawson, who attended the Unite the Right rally in Charlottesville, Virginia, in 2017, easily defeated his Democrat opponent in the state’s 10th District State House race.

Many states also passed ballot measures that could further far-right agendas, including anti-transgender rules, onerous voter ID laws, and anti-immigrant measures.

“Arizona, Colorado, Nebraska, and Washington approved bans on trans athletes in female sports, so girls in Washington state, for instance, will now have to go through a genital exam or pass a DNA test before they can participate in youth sports,” Devin Burghart, executive director with IREHR, said on Friday during a briefing with reporters.

The success of these ballot measures was celebrated by extremist groups as evidence that Americans wanted what the current GOP could not deliver.

The Proud Boys, the street-fighting group that Immigration and Customs Enforcement operations made largely redundant in recent years, were optimistic following the losses. “The ultimate rebuke of the current Republican establishment was citizens voting them out of office, then doing their job for them by passing ballot measures on issues the Republicans should have already handled,” the Cape Fear Proud Boys wrote in their Telegram channel.

“What this signals to me is even in those places where they lost, they're getting 41-47 percent of the vote, [so] there's still a mass base to try to be mobilized,” Chuck Tanner, research director at IREHR, said during the briefing. “That's the lesson that some of these groups are taking out of it, is that there's still that mass base.”

And it is the existence of this base, extremist groups claim, that leaves the Republican Party vulnerable to takeover from the inside.

“Now is the time, if you're a nationalist, to start looking at moving on the Republican Party where you live,” Warren Balogh, a white supremacist from Pittsburgh who attended the Unite the Right rally in Charlottesville in 2017, wrote on his Telegram channel. “And by that I mean, organizing your guys and going in to do a hostile takeover.”

“This may be the best thing that has happened to the Republican Party in a half-century,” the Cape Fear Proud Boys declared in a post on Telegram days after the election. “Now you will learn to placate us, or you will continue to fail. Understand this: You’re going to run people we like, and you’re going to do what we say.”

But other extremists appear less inclined to wait until the next general election in 2028, preferring a more direct takeover.

"I'm not saying that we will win through democracy. I'm just saying we'll win,” Joel Webbon, a Christian nationalist pastor said last week in a video posted online after the election. Webbon and fellow far-right pastors, as well as guest speaker and noted conspiracy theorist Alex Jones, are set to meet at a conference in Texas this week where attendees will discuss, in at least one talk, “battle plans” for “Christian nationalists on the right to be able to seize power.”

Experts WIRED spoke to said they have not seen any explicit calls for violence from the far-right groups they are tracking, but some figures are already looking ahead to January 3, when Congress is set to certify the midterm election results.

Far-right activist and pardoned January 6 rioter Jake Lang, however, is planning an event in DC on January 3. It is unclear how many people are planning to attend, but one of the Christian nationalist pastors listed by Lang as an official speaker has said he won’t be speaking.

Lang, who spent last weekend disrupting a cat convention that he thought was a furry convention, did not respond to a request for comment.

U.S. Marine Calls Alaska Airlines Employee “Anti-Military” After Being Denied A Free Upgrade

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

A United States Marine Corps member says an Alaska Airlines employee was “anti-military” after declining his request for a free extra-legroom upgrade. He also alleges that the employee was rude and accused him of lying about previous upgrades. Those are separate complaints, and I think they deserve separate answers. Everyone deserves courteous service. Military service, however, does not entitle someone to a better seat.

The Instagram algorithim placed Taylor Yontz at the top of my feed this morning, where he describes an encounter at Portland International Airport. According to his account, he saw roughly 30 unassigned seats on the seat map and requested a complimentary move to an extra-legroom seat. He says he is 6 feet 4 inches tall, regularly makes this request, and receives an upgrade about nine times out of 10.

This time, he says, the employee told him free military upgrades were against policy. He alleges the agent spoke with disgust and accused him of lying when he mentioned previous complimentary upgrades. The employee offered a paid move, which he declined at $150.

You can listen to his side of the story here:

Alaska publishes a substantial list of military and veteran benefits. These include:

Complimentary upgrades based solely on military service are not among the listed benefits. So at the outset, there is no policy for upgrading service members when space is available, which even Yontz concedes.

I don’t blame him for asking, especially because he is 6’4″. Sitting in economy class is difficult enough for an average-sized person…it’s brutal for someone that tall.

But “it can’t hurt to ask” needs to leave room for the employee to decline. Otherwise, it becomes a test the agent can pass only by giving something away. Yontz strikes me as highly-entiteld to suggest that he should be given this upgrade because he has in the past, as if it’s standard procedure.

We’ll talk about the Alaska Airlines employee below, but by arguing that you’ve been upgraded in the past and therefore you should today, entitlement is on full display.

If an agent actually accused him of lying about previous upgrades, I would find that inappropriate (I’m not convinced, but it is certainly possible). The employee should have politely explained the current policy without assumptions about past interaction the passenger has had with the airline.

There is no contradiction between “another employee upgraded me before” and “I cannot provide that upgrade today” and Alaska should examine his complaint about insulting language on its own merits.

But even if the employee handled the conversation poorly, that would establish poor service, not necessarily hostility toward military members. And even if it was one agent being hostile (again, certainly possible), it’s still a different matter than whether the upgrade should have been denied.

I appreciate the sacrifices military members and their families make. Airlines can recognize those sacrifices through practical benefits, and Alaska offers several. But I hate to see that appreciation reduced to whether an airport employee hands over a seat upgrade…Yontz’s conduct is unbecoming of a member of the U.S. military (and I was one too, thank you very much).

If one agent makes an exception, the next agent should not face an accusation of prejudice for following the published rules. That creates an incentive to stop offering discretionary favors altogether. Accepting a kind gesture with gratitude leaves room for someone else to receive one later. Treating it as the minimum acceptable response makes it harder for employees to exercise generosity without creating expectations their colleagues must then manage.

The practical result of this whole brouhaha should be a memo to gate agents that no one is to be upgraded for free.

If the Alaska Airlines employee was insulting, the passenger has every reason to complain about that conduct. He did not need to receive an upgrade to deserve a respectful conversation.

But declining a complimentary extra-legroom seat does not, on its own, make an employee anti-military. Alaska’s published benefits do not promise that upgrade, and previous exceptions do not change that.

To all travelers: ask politely, enjoy the upgrade if it happens, but if it does not, a declined upgrade is not evidence that someone disrespects you, no matter what uniform you wear.

Ripple Prime Head Marc Asch to Step Down by the End of 2026

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The Head of Ripple Prime, Marc Asch, is stepping down from the company by the end of 2015, according to a CoinDesk report citing a Ripple spokesperson.

Noel Kimmel, currently a Senior Vice President at Ripple Prime, will replace him in that role.

Ripple Prime was officially introduced after Ripple acquired Hidden Road in October 2025 for $1.25 billion, rebranding the latter.

Marc Asch founded Hidden Road in 2018 as a prime brokerage for institutional clients. Before that, Asch worked at SAC Capital.

Ripple Prime focused on institutional investment across several classes, including digital assets. Through Ripple Prime, the company also created new exposure to its native stablecoin, RLUSD.

That same month, Ripple also completed another $1 billion acquisition of a stablecoin platform, GTreasury, adding to its renewed financial ecosystem.

Recently, Ripple Prime has won the Prime Broker of the Year: EMEA award at the 2026 Hedgeweek US Awards.

The Hedgeweek US Awards is an annual event that honors and recognizes companies for their efficiency and contributions across several categories.

Some of these categories include custody, trading infrastructure, blockchain technology, compliance, and decentralized finance (DeFi).

The award has marked growing recognition of Ripple as a top brokerage firm in the world of finance.
